<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct an ergodic invertible transformation of a standard nonatomic probability space with zero Kolmogorov–Sinai entropy that has no smooth positive-volume model. More precisely, it is not measurably conjugate to any <i>C</i><sup>∞</sup> diffeomorphism preserving a strictly positive smooth probability density on a compact finite-dimensional manifold. A single example excludes every finite dimension, including models on nonorientable manifolds and manifolds with smooth boundary.

- **claim-universal-smooth-volume-obstruction** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There exists an ergodic invertible measure-preserving transformation $(X,\mu,T)$ of a standard nonatomic probability space such that $h_\mu(T)=0$ and $(X,\mu,T)$ is not measurably conjugate, on invariant conull sets, to any $C^\infty$ diffeomorphism of a compact finite-dimensional manifold preserving a strictly positive smooth probability density. The manifold may have smooth boundary and need not be orientable.
  - Explanation: A single standard nonatomic ergodic invertible system has zero entropy and excludes smooth positive-volume models in every finite dimension, including manifolds with smooth boundary and nonorientable manifolds.
- **claim-dyadic-chord-summability** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-dyadic)): For the process above, put $$v_l=\mathbb EA_{l-1}^2-\mathbb EA_l^2,\qquad l\geq 1.$$ Then $v_l\geq 0$, $\sum_{l\geq 1}v_l\leq\mathbb EY_0^2$, and $$\label{eq:dyadic-convolution} \\|Z_h\\|_{L^2} \leq \frac12\sum_{a=0}^{h-1}2^{-a/2}\sqrt{v_{h-a}} \qquad(h\geq 1).$$ Consequently $$\label{eq:dyadic-square-sum} \sum_{h\geq 1}\\|Z_h\\|_{L^2}^2 \leq C\,\mathbb EY_0^2$$ for an absolute constant $C$. The same estimates hold for a block beginning at any deterministic integer time.
  - Explanation: Stationarity and a finite second moment give square-summable normalized chord deviations without independence or an ergodic convergence rate.
- **claim-good-finite-orbit-segments** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-good-frames)): For each $\eta>0$ there is a positive sequence $(b_k)_{k\geq1}$ with $$\label{eq:b-summability} b_k\geq 2^{-k/10}, \qquad \sum_{k\geq1}b_k^2<\infty,$$ and compact sets $\widetilde{\mathcal G}_k\subset\mathcal F M$ and $\mathcal G_k=\pi(\widetilde{\mathcal G}_k)\subset M$ such that $$\widetilde\nu(\widetilde{\mathcal G}_k)\geq1-\eta/100, \qquad \nu(\mathcal G_k)\geq1-\eta/100.$$ For $n=2^k$, $b=b_k$, and $e\in\widetilde{\mathcal G}_k$, define $$A_t=R(\widetilde S^t e)\quad(-n\leq t<n), \qquad \lambda_j=\frac1{2n}\sum_{t=-n}^{n-1}\ln(A_t)_{jj}.$$ Then for every $j$ and every integer $-n\leq u\leq n$, $$\label{eq:good-chord} \left\|\sum_{t=-n}^{u-1}\ln(A_t)_{jj} -(u+n)\lambda_j\right\|\leq bn.$$ Consequently, on every subinterval $-n\leq u\leq v\leq n$, $$\label{eq:good-subinterval} \left\|\sum_{t=u}^{v-1}\ln(A_t)_{jj} -(v-u)\lambda_j\right\|\leq2bn.$$
  - Explanation: Large compact sets of frame paths satisfy simultaneous diagonal chord estimates with a square-summable error sequence. The same rates control every subinterval.
- **claim-finite-triangular-splitting** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-finite-splitting)): Fix $d\geq1$ and $A_*>1$. There is a constant $C$, depending only on these two quantities, with the following properties. Let $n\geq2$, $b>0$, and $\ln(2n+1)\leq bn$. Suppose that $A_t$, $-n\leq t<n$, are upper triangular with positive diagonal and $$\\|A_t\\|,\ \\|A_t^{-1}\\|\leq A_*, \qquad \left\|\sum_{t=-n}^{u-1}\ln(A_t)_{jj} -(u+n)\lambda_j\right\|\leq bn$$ for $-n\leq u\leq n$ and $1\leq j\leq d$, where $\lambda_j=(2n)^{-1}\sum_{t=-n}^{n-1}\ln(A_t)_{jj}$.* *First, order the rates as $\lambda_{(1)}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{(d)}$. For every $0\leq q\leq d$ and every $-n\leq s\leq t\leq n$, $$\label{eq:exterior-rate-comparison} \left\|\ln\\|\mathord{\bigwedge}^q A(t,s)\\| -(t-s)\sum_{j=1}^q\lambda_{(j)}\right\|\leq Cbn.$$ The analogous estimate for $A(s,t)$ uses the $q$ largest members of $\{-\lambda_1,\ldots,-\lambda_d\}$.* *Next, suppose that real $\gamma$ and $g>0$ separate all the rates: each $\lambda_j$ is at most $\gamma-g$ or at least $\gamma+g$. Let $F$ be the set of indices in the second group, let $p=\|F\|$, and put $$\Lambda=\sum_{j\in F}\lambda_j,\qquad \Omega=-\sum_{j\notin F}\lambda_j.$$ There are decompositions $\mathbb R^d=E_t^s\oplus E_t^f$, $-n\leq t\leq n$, invariant under each $A_t$, with $\dim E_t^f=p$, whose two projections have norm at most $e^{Cbn}$. For $s\leq t$ and $l=t-s$ they satisfy $$\begin{aligned} \\|A(t,s)\|E_s^s\\|&\leq e^{(\gamma-g)l+Cbn}, & \\|A(s,t)\|E_t^f\\|&\leq e^{-(\gamma+g)l+Cbn}, \label{eq:split-propagation}\\ \\|\mathord{\bigwedge}^p A(t,s)\\|&\leq e^{\Lambda l+Cbn}, & e^{\Lambda l-Cbn}\leq J_p(A(t,s)\|E_s^f)&\leq e^{\Lambda l+Cbn}, \label{eq:fast-volume}\\ \\|\mathord{\bigwedge}^{d-p} A(s,t)\\|&\leq e^{\Omega l+Cbn}, & e^{\Omega l-Cbn}\leq J_{d-p}(A(s,t)\|E_t^s)&\leq e^{\Omega l+Cbn}. \label{eq:slow-backward-volume} \end{aligned}$$ If $0<p<d$ and at least one of $v_1,\ldots,v_p$ belongs to $E_s^s$, then $$\label{eq:mixed-exterior} \\|A(t,s)v_1\wedge\cdots\wedge A(t,s)v_p\\| \leq e^{(\Lambda-2g)l+Cbn}\prod_{j=1}^p\\|v_j\\|.$$ The backward analogue has exterior degree $d-p$, exponent $(\Omega-2g)l+Cbn$, and at least one factor in $E_t^f$. When a group is empty its space is $\{0\}$, all statements on that space are vacuous, and the exterior-degree-zero conventions apply.
  - Explanation: Triangular products admit exterior growth estimates on every subinterval. A separated rate threshold additionally gives invariant slow and fast spaces, controlled projections, intrinsic volume estimates, and a loss for mixed exterior factors.
- **claim-common-bin-spaces** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-bin-spaces)): For all sufficiently large $k$, each spatial bin $U$ meeting $\mathcal G_k$ has a number $$\gamma_U\in[g,(8d+1)g]$$ such that every good witness frame over $U$ has all its rates at most $\gamma_U-g$ or at least $\gamma_U+g$, with the same number $p_U$ in the second group. Apply [prop:finite-splitting](#prop:finite-splitting) using this threshold. There is $c>0$, independent of the bin and the witnesses, such that:* 1. *The spaces $E_0^s$ from any two good witnesses over $U$ are within $e^{-c gL}$ of one another in its coordinate chart. The same holds for the spaces $E_0^f$.* 2. *For good paths starting in $U$ whose time-$n$ endpoints lie in a common endpoint bin $V$, their spaces $E_n^f$ are within $e^{-c gL}$ of one another in the chart of $V$. For paths whose time-$(-n)$ endpoints lie in a common bin, the same assertion holds for $E_{-n}^s$.* *In particular, fix one representative space for each nonempty combination of bins in <span class="upright">(ii)</span>. Orthogonal projection onto it, restricted to any corresponding actual endpoint space, is an isomorphism with inverse norm at most $2$. If $p_U=0$ or $p_U=d$, the relevant zero or full spaces agree exactly.
  - Explanation: A spatial bin admits a common rate threshold and fast dimension for all good witnesses. Short derivative comparisons align their starting and endpoint spaces sufficiently accurately for fixed endpoint projections.
- **claim-relative-exterior-stability** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-relative-exterior)): Fix $d\geq1$ and $K\geq1$. Let $0\leq p\leq d$, let $m\geq1$, and let $A_0,\ldots,A_{m-1}$ and $\widehat A_0,\ldots,\widehat A_{m-1}$ be real $d$-by-$d$ matrices such that $$\\|A_t\\|\leq K,\qquad \\|\widehat A_t-A_t\\|\leq\epsilon\leq1.$$ Use the same product notation as above for nonnegative indices. Suppose $a\geq0$, $\sigma\in\mathbb R$, and $$\\|\mathord{\bigwedge}^p A(t,s)\\| \leq e^{\sigma(t-s)+a} \qquad(0\leq s\leq t\leq m).$$ There is $C=C(d,K)$ such that $$\label{eq:exterior-perturbation} \left\\|\mathord{\bigwedge}^p\widehat A(m,0) -\mathord{\bigwedge}^p A(m,0)\right\\| \leq e^{\sigma m+a} \left[(1+C\epsilon e^{a+\|\sigma\|})^m-1\right].$$ For $p=0$ the left side is zero.* *In particular, use the scales <a href="#eq:smooth-scales" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:smooth-scales">[eq:smooth-scales]</a>, take $m\leq2n$, $a\leq C_1bn$, $\|\sigma\|\leq C_2$, and $\epsilon\leq C_3e^{-3Bn/2}$, with fixed constants. Then for every fixed $C_0>0$ the left side of <a href="#eq:exterior-perturbation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exterior-perturbation">[eq:exterior-perturbation]</a> is $$\label{eq:relative-exterior-small} o\!\left(e^{\sigma m-C_0bn}\right),$$ uniformly for all matrices satisfying these hypotheses.
  - Explanation: Expanding a perturbed exterior product by insertion positions yields a relative error estimate. At the prescribed smooth scales this error remains negligible even when the leading volume is small.
- **claim-smooth-switching-law** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-switching)): Let $M$ be a compact smooth manifold of positive finite dimension, let $\nu$ be a smooth positive probability volume in the density sense of [sec:introduction](#sec:introduction), and let $S:M\to M$ be a smooth diffeomorphism preserving $\nu$. Fix a Riemannian distance $d_M$ and $\eta>0$. There exist positive numbers $B_k$ with $\sum_k B_k^{16}<\infty$ and a constant $C$ such that the following holds for all sufficiently large $k$, with $n=2^k$.* *There is a finite measurable partition $\mathcal D_k$ of $M$ with $\#\mathcal D_k\le \exp(CB_kn)$. Define the probability measure $$\label{eq:pair-law} \rho_k=\sum_{\substack{Q\in\mathcal D_k\\\nu(Q)>0}} \frac{(\nu\|_Q)\otimes(\nu\|_Q)}{\nu(Q)}.$$ Thus one first samples a cell with its $\nu$-weight and then samples two independent points in that cell. There are a measurable set $G_k\subset M\times M$, with $\rho_k(G_k)\ge1-\eta$, and a measurable map $z_k:G_k\to M$ such that $$\label{eq:switch-law} (z_k)_*(\rho_k\|_{G_k})\le 2\nu$$ and $$\label{eq:switch-shadow} \sup_{(x,y)\in G_k}\left[ \max_{0\le t\le n}d_M(S^t z_k(x,y),S^t x) +\max_{-n\le t\le0}d_M(S^t z_k(x,y),S^t y) \right]\longrightarrow0.$$ The measure on the left of <a href="#eq:switch-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:switch-law">[eq:switch-law]</a> is not normalized after restriction to $G_k$. The same conclusions hold if $M$ has a smooth boundary.
  - Explanation: A partition of controlled size permits switching the future and past of most conditionally independent same-cell pairs. The retained output law is dominated by twice the invariant volume without renormalization.
- **claim-nonlinear-orbit-joins** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-switch-orbits)): For every sufficiently large $k$ and every $(x,y)\in G_k$, the first two equations of <a href="#eq:four-switch-equations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:four-switch-equations">[eq:four-switch-equations]</a> have a solution $z$ with $$\label{eq:sharp-shadow} \max_{0\le t\le n}d_M(S^tz,S^tx) +\max_{-n\le t\le0}d_M(S^tz,S^ty) \le e^{Cgn}r.$$ In fixed regular coordinates along these halves the solution is unique among displacement arrays of sup norm at most $\delta$. The complementary solution $w$ has the analogous properties with $x,y$ exchanged. In the product coordinates of the common cell, if $x=(x_s,x_f)$ and $y=(y_s,y_f)$, then $$\label{eq:ideal-switch} z=(y_s,x_f)+o(r),\qquad w=(x_s,y_f)+o(r),$$ uniformly over all retained pairs. Both outputs remain in the original product cell, and their relevant endpoints remain in the respective four original endpoint bins.
  - Explanation: A weighted boundary inverse and contraction produce complementary orbit joins. A larger uniqueness tube and strict wall margins preserve the original product cell and endpoint labels.
- **claim-switch-pair-jacobian** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-switch-jacobian)): All four derivatives $DH_{xx}(x)$, $DH_{yy}(y)$, $DH_{xy}(z)$, and $DH_{yx}(w)$ are nonsingular. Locally the equations <a href="#eq:four-switch-equations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:four-switch-equations">[eq:four-switch-equations]</a> define a smooth pair map $\Phi(x,y)=(z,w)$ with $$\label{eq:pair-jacobian} J_{\nu\otimes\nu}\Phi(x,y)=1+o(1),$$ uniformly on the retained pairs. The conclusion includes empty slow or fast groups.
  - Explanation: Relative control of leading endpoint minors makes the endpoint maps nonsingular. In the complementary pair map their expansion factors cancel, giving an asymptotically volume-preserving Jacobian.
- **claim-global-switch-injectivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-switch-injective)): The map $\Phi:G_k\to M\times M$, $\Phi(x,y)=(z,w)$, is measurable and injective. Its restriction to countably many measurable pieces agrees with smooth local diffeomorphisms having the Jacobian in <a href="#eq:pair-jacobian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pair-jacobian">[eq:pair-jacobian]</a>.
  - Explanation: Output cell and endpoint labels recover the defining equations. Boundary uniqueness then recovers both inputs, while countably many smooth local branches establish measurability and justify change of variables.
- **claim-finite-word-families** (construction, [source](#stmt-word-frequency)): Choose $s_1$ so that $K_1\ge256$, and define the parameters by <a href="#eq:word-parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:word-parameters">[eq:word-parameters]</a>–<a href="#eq:word-recursion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:word-recursion">[eq:word-recursion]</a>. There are families $\mathcal W_i\subset\mathcal A^{m_i}$ of cardinality $L_i$, with $\mathcal W_1=\mathcal A$, such that every $w\in\mathcal W_{i+1}$ is a concatenation of $N_i$ members of $\mathcal W_i$ and the following properties hold.* 1. ***Relations within groups.** Write $c_i(w)=(u_0,\ldots,u_{N_i-1})$. For every $0\le b<N_i/H_i$, every $0\le k\le\lfloor K_i/8\rfloor$, and every $0\le r<r+2^k<H_i$, $$[u_{bH_i+r},u_{bH_i+r+2^k}]_i=0.$$* 2. ***Distance between words and shifted words.** If $v,w\in\mathcal W_{i+1}$ are distinct, then $$d_{\mathrm H}\bigl(c_i(v),c_i(w)\bigr) \ge(1-\varepsilon_i)N_i.$$ For all $v,w,z\in\mathcal W_{i+1}$, with repetitions allowed, and all $1\le t<N_i$, $$d_{\mathrm H}\bigl((c_i(v)c_i(w))[t,t+N_i),c_i(z)\bigr) \ge(1-\varepsilon_i)N_i.$$* 3. ***Distributions of short windows.** Let $U$ be uniform on $\mathcal W_{i+1}$. For each $0\le t\le N_i-D_i^*$ there is a probability $Q_{i,t}$ on $\mathcal W_i^{D_i^*}$ such that $$\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}\bigl(\mathcal L(c_i(U)[t,t+D_i^*)),Q_{i,t}\bigr) \le\vartheta.$$ Moreover, for $1\le d'\le D_i^*$, any distinct positions $0\le r_1<\cdots<r_{d'}<D_i^*$, and any prescribed labels $v_1,\ldots,v_{d'}\in\mathcal W_i$, $$\label{eq:word-sublist} Q_{i,t}\{u:u_{r_j}=v_j\text{ for }1\le j\le d'\} \le 2\left(\frac{2^{2K_i}}{L_i}\right)^{d'}.$$* 4. ***Approximation in each individual word.** Suppose $i\ge2$. For every fixed $w\in\mathcal W_{i+1}$, write $c_i(w)=(u_0,\ldots,u_{N_i-1})$ and let $J$ be uniform on $\{0,\ldots,N_i-1\}$. If $U$ is uniform on $\mathcal W_i$, then for each $0\le t\le N_{i-1}-D_{i-1}^*$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}\left( \mathcal L\bigl(c_{i-1}(u_J)[t,t+D_{i-1}^*)\bigr), \mathcal L\bigl(c_{i-1}(U)[t,t+D_{i-1}^*)\bigr) \right)\le\vartheta.$$ The first law averages the constituents of the single fixed word $w$; it does not average over $\mathcal W_{i+1}$.
  - Explanation: Finite families simultaneously satisfy orthogonality at prescribed lags, separation from distinct and shifted words, high-information short-window laws, and approximation inside every individual parent.
- **claim-probability-vector-average** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-word-tv)): Let $Y_1,\ldots,Y_q$ be independent random probability vectors on a set of cardinality $A$, all with mean $p$. Then $$\mathbb E\,\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}\left(\frac1q\sum_{b=1}^qY_b,p\right) \le\frac12\sqrt{\frac Aq}.$$ Coordinates within an individual vector need not be independent.
  - Explanation: Independence between probability vectors bounds the expected total variation error of their average. Dependence between coordinates of an individual vector is allowed.
- **claim-symbol-recognition** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-recognition)): For the families in [prop:words](#prop:words), put $$d_i=\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}(1-\varepsilon_j),\qquad d_*=\prod_{j=1}^{\infty}(1-\varepsilon_j)>0.$$ Distinct $v,w\in\mathcal W_i$ satisfy $$d_{\mathrm H}(v,w)\ge d_i m_i\ge d_*m_i.$$ For all $v,w,z\in\mathcal W_i$ and every $1\le t<m_i$, $$d_{\mathrm H}\bigl((vw)[t,t+m_i),z\bigr) \ge d_i m_i\ge d_*m_i.$$ These distances count symbols in $\mathcal A$, and repeated source words are allowed.
  - Explanation: Induction transfers constituent-label distances to a uniform positive symbol distance, including every nontrivial splice offset and repeated source words.
- **claim-word-rate-budget** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rates)): Let $R_i=\ell_i/m_i$. Every $m_i$ is a power of two, with $$\log m_i=\sum_{j<i}K_j.$$ The rates satisfy $$\label{eq:word-rate-decay} R_{i+1}\le\frac{\varepsilon_i}{100}R_i, \qquad R_i\longrightarrow0.$$ They also have the lower bound $$\label{eq:word-rate-lower} R_i\ge R_1\,200^{-(i-1)} 2^{-(i-1)(i+4)/2} \ge R_1\,2^{-(i-1)(i+20)/2},$$ and $$\label{eq:word-rate-divergence} \sum_{i=1}^{\infty}K_iR_i^{16}=\infty.$$
  - Explanation: Word lengths are dyadic and entropy per symbol tends to zero. Its lower bound and the much faster growth of the scale parameters nevertheless force divergence of the weighted rate budget.
- **claim-ergodic-zero-entropy-system** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-system)): There is a $T$-invariant ergodic Borel probability $\mu$ on $X$. Every $T$-invariant Borel probability on $X$ is nonatomic, is standard modulo null sets, and has zero Kolmogorov–Sinai entropy.
  - Explanation: The compact phase extension admits an ergodic invariant probability. Every invariant probability is nonatomic and standard and has zero entropy, regardless of its word frequencies.
- **claim-aligned-boundary-law** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-aligned-law)): The law of <a href="#eq:aligned-list" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:aligned-list">[eq:aligned-list]</a> under $\mu$ is the law under $\mu_i$ of the first $D-1$ level-$i$ words beginning at zero. Under $\mu_i$, the index $J=a_{i+2}/m_i$ is uniform on $\{0,\ldots,N_iN_{i+1}-1\}$ and is independent of $V_{i+2}$. More precisely, for each such $j$ and $v\in\mathcal W_{i+2}$, $$\label{eq:parent-slot-law} \mu_i(J=j,V_{i+2}=v) =\frac{\mu_{i+2}(V_{i+2}=v)}{N_iN_{i+1}}.$$
  - Explanation: Advancing to the first aligned boundary gives the boundary-conditioned list law. Within a parent, the constituent slot is uniform independently of the parent label.
- **claim-aligned-window-entropy** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-window-entropy)): For all sufficiently large $i$, for every power of two $2\le D\le D_i^*$, and every integer $t$, $$\label{eq:window-entropy} H_\mu(\mathcal L_{i,D}(\,\cdot\,,t)) \ge .996(D-1)\ell_i,\qquad \ell_i=\log L_i.$$ The large-$i$ threshold is independent of $D,t$ and of the invariant probability on $X$.
  - Explanation: The per-parent approximation and short-window atom bound yield nearly maximal entropy for aligned lists. The threshold is uniform in the window length, time, and invariant probability.
- **claim-legal-window-graph-relations** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-graph-windows)): Let $2\le D\le D_i^*$ be a power of two and set $n=m_iD$. For all sufficiently large $i$, with $\mu$-probability at least $$1-\alpha_{i,D},\qquad \alpha_{i,D}=\frac{2D-2}{H_i},$$ all corresponding entries of $\mathcal L_{i,D}(x,-n)$ and $\mathcal L_{i,D}(x,0)$ pass the level-$i$ graph test simultaneously. In particular $\sup_{D\le D_i^*}\alpha_{i,D}\to0$.
  - Explanation: Corresponding past and future entries have an allowed dyadic lag. All graph relations hold together unless their combined span crosses a group boundary.
- **claim-cheap-conditioning-scales** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cheap-scales)): Let $(B_k)_{k\ge1}$ be any positive sequence with $\sum_k B_k^{16}<\infty$. There exist levels $i\to\infty$ and powers of two $D$ satisfying $$2^{\lceil K_i/16\rceil}\le D\le2^{\lfloor K_i/8\rfloor}$$ such that, for $n=m_iD$, $$\label{eq:relative-cost} \frac{B_{\log n}}{R_i}\longrightarrow0, \qquad R_i=\frac{\ell_i}{m_i}.$$ In particular any partitions with $\log\#\mathcal D_k\le C B_k2^k$ obey $$\label{eq:cheap-partition} \log\#\mathcal D_{\log n}=o((D-1)\ell_i)$$ on this sequence, for every fixed $C$.
  - Explanation: Summability of smooth costs and divergence of the symbolic rate budget select arbitrarily late dyadic tests where cell conditioning loses a negligible fraction of the list entropy.
- **claim-independent-graph-passing-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-graph-independent)): Let $L=2^{2s}-1$ and equip the nonzero vectors of $\mathbb F_2^{2s}$ with a nondegenerate alternating form. Suppose a family of pairs of laws $(p_\xi,q_\xi)$ on these vectors is indexed by a probability space, and $$\mathbb E_\xi H(p_\xi)\ge .99\log L,\qquad \mathbb E_\xi H(q_\xi)\ge .99\log L.$$ For all sufficiently large $L$, independent samples from the two laws, followed by averaging over $\xi$, pass the test $[u,v]=0$ with probability at most $.62$.
  - Explanation: Average entropy bounds control the heavy parts of the input laws. A character-matrix operator bound controls their light product and bounds the averaged passing probability for independent samples.
- **claim-shadowing-symbol-agreement** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-symbol-matching)): For all sufficiently large dyadic times in the smooth switching estimate, the retained pairs for which $$\begin{aligned} \#\{0\le t<n:f(S^tz)\ne f(S^tx)\} &<d_*n/100,\label{eq:future-symbol-match}\\ \#\{-n\le t<0:f(S^tz)\ne f(S^ty)\} &<d_*n/100\label{eq:past-symbol-match} \end{aligned}$$ have original $\rho$-mass at least $.96$.
  - Explanation: Separated compact cores of the fixed symbol partition convert uniform orbit shadowing into symbol agreement on most retained pairs. Output-law domination controls visits outside the cores.

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-finite-splitting",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $d\\geq1$ and $A_*>1$. There is a constant $C$, depending only on these two quantities, with the following properties. Let $n\\geq2$, $b>0$, and $\\ln(2n+1)\\leq bn$. Suppose that $A_t$, $-n\\leq t<n$, are upper triangular with positive diagonal and $$\\|A_t\\|,\\ \\|A_t^{-1}\\|\\leq A_*,\n \\qquad\n \\left|\\sum_{t=-n}^{u-1}\\ln(A_t)_{jj}\n                  -(u+n)\\lambda_j\\right|\\leq bn$$ for $-n\\leq u\\leq n$ and $1\\leq j\\leq d$, where $\\lambda_j=(2n)^{-1}\\sum_{t=-n}^{n-1}\\ln(A_t)_{jj}$.*\n\n\n*First, order the rates as $\\lambda_{(1)}\\geq\\cdots\\geq\\lambda_{(d)}$. For every $0\\leq q\\leq d$ and every $-n\\leq s\\leq t\\leq n$, $$\\label{eq:exterior-rate-comparison}\n \\left|\\ln\\|\\mathord{\\bigwedge}^q A(t,s)\\|\n       -(t-s)\\sum_{j=1}^q\\lambda_{(j)}\\right|\\leq Cbn.$$ The analogous estimate for $A(s,t)$ uses the $q$ largest members of $\\{-\\lambda_1,\\ldots,-\\lambda_d\\}$.*\n\n\n\n\n\n*Next, suppose that real $\\gamma$ and $g>0$ separate all the rates: each $\\lambda_j$ is at most $\\gamma-g$ or at least $\\gamma+g$. Let $F$ be the set of indices in the second group, let $p=|F|$, and put $$\\Lambda=\\sum_{j\\in F}\\lambda_j,\\qquad\n \\Omega=-\\sum_{j\\notin F}\\lambda_j.$$ There are decompositions $\\mathbb R^d=E_t^s\\oplus E_t^f$, $-n\\leq t\\leq n$, invariant under each $A_t$, with $\\dim E_t^f=p$, whose two projections have norm at most $e^{Cbn}$. For $s\\leq t$ and $l=t-s$ they satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|A(t,s)|E_s^s\\|&\\leq e^{(\\gamma-g)l+Cbn},\n &\n \\|A(s,t)|E_t^f\\|&\\leq e^{-(\\gamma+g)l+Cbn},\n \\label{eq:split-propagation}\\\\\n \\|\\mathord{\\bigwedge}^p A(t,s)\\|&\\leq e^{\\Lambda l+Cbn},\n &\n e^{\\Lambda l-Cbn}\\leq\n J_p(A(t,s)|E_s^f)&\\leq e^{\\Lambda l+Cbn},\n \\label{eq:fast-volume}\\\\\n \\|\\mathord{\\bigwedge}^{d-p} A(s,t)\\|&\\leq e^{\\Omega l+Cbn},\n &\n e^{\\Omega l-Cbn}\\leq\n J_{d-p}(A(s,t)|E_t^s)&\\leq e^{\\Omega l+Cbn}.\n \\label{eq:slow-backward-volume}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If $0<p<d$ and at least one of $v_1,\\ldots,v_p$ belongs to $E_s^s$, then $$\\label{eq:mixed-exterior}\n \\|A(t,s)v_1\\wedge\\cdots\\wedge A(t,s)v_p\\|\n \\leq e^{(\\Lambda-2g)l+Cbn}\\prod_{j=1}^p\\|v_j\\|.$$ The backward analogue has exterior degree $d-p$, exponent $(\\Omega-2g)l+Cbn$, and at least one factor in $E_t^f$. When a group is empty its space is $\\{0\\}$, all statements on that space are vacuous, and the exterior-degree-zero conventions apply.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-bin-spaces",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $k$, each spatial bin $U$ meeting $\\mathcal G_k$ has a number $$\\gamma_U\\in[g,(8d+1)g]$$ such that every good witness frame over $U$ has all its rates at most $\\gamma_U-g$ or at least $\\gamma_U+g$, with the same number $p_U$ in the second group. Apply [prop:finite-splitting](#prop:finite-splitting) using this threshold. There is $c>0$, independent of the bin and the witnesses, such that:*\n\n1.  *The spaces $E_0^s$ from any two good witnesses over $U$ are within $e^{-c gL}$ of one another in its coordinate chart. The same holds for the spaces $E_0^f$.*\n\n2.  *For good paths starting in $U$ whose time-$n$ endpoints lie in a common endpoint bin $V$, their spaces $E_n^f$ are within $e^{-c gL}$ of one another in the chart of $V$. For paths whose time-$(-n)$ endpoints lie in a common bin, the same assertion holds for $E_{-n}^s$.*\n\n*In particular, fix one representative space for each nonempty combination of bins in <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span>. Orthogonal projection onto it, restricted to any corresponding actual endpoint space, is an isomorphism with inverse norm at most $2$. If $p_U=0$ or $p_U=d$, the relevant zero or full spaces agree exactly.",
        "source_id": "PM-152.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-relative-exterior",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $d\\geq1$ and $K\\geq1$. Let $0\\leq p\\leq d$, let $m\\geq1$, and let $A_0,\\ldots,A_{m-1}$ and $\\widehat A_0,\\ldots,\\widehat A_{m-1}$ be real $d$-by-$d$ matrices such that $$\\|A_t\\|\\leq K,\\qquad\n \\|\\widehat A_t-A_t\\|\\leq\\epsilon\\leq1.$$ Use the same product notation as above for nonnegative indices. Suppose $a\\geq0$, $\\sigma\\in\\mathbb R$, and $$\\|\\mathord{\\bigwedge}^p A(t,s)\\|\n       \\leq e^{\\sigma(t-s)+a}\n \\qquad(0\\leq s\\leq t\\leq m).$$ There is $C=C(d,K)$ such that $$\\label{eq:exterior-perturbation}\n \\left\\|\\mathord{\\bigwedge}^p\\widehat A(m,0)\n             -\\mathord{\\bigwedge}^p A(m,0)\\right\\|\n \\leq e^{\\sigma m+a}\n       \\left[(1+C\\epsilon e^{a+|\\sigma|})^m-1\\right].$$ For $p=0$ the left side is zero.*\n\n*In particular, use the scales <a href=\"#eq:smooth-scales\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:smooth-scales\">[eq:smooth-scales]</a>, take $m\\leq2n$, $a\\leq C_1bn$, $|\\sigma|\\leq C_2$, and $\\epsilon\\leq C_3e^{-3Bn/2}$, with fixed constants. Then for every fixed $C_0>0$ the left side of <a href=\"#eq:exterior-perturbation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exterior-perturbation\">[eq:exterior-perturbation]</a> is $$\\label{eq:relative-exterior-small}\n o\\!\\left(e^{\\sigma m-C_0bn}\\right),$$ uniformly for all matrices satisfying these hypotheses.",
        "source_id": "PM-152.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-relative-exterior",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $d\\geq1$ and $K\\geq1$. Let $0\\leq p\\leq d$, let $m\\geq1$, and let $A_0,\\ldots,A_{m-1}$ and $\\widehat A_0,\\ldots,\\widehat A_{m-1}$ be real $d$-by-$d$ matrices such that $$\\|A_t\\|\\leq K,\\qquad\n \\|\\widehat A_t-A_t\\|\\leq\\epsilon\\leq1.$$ Use the same product notation as above for nonnegative indices. Suppose $a\\geq0$, $\\sigma\\in\\mathbb R$, and $$\\|\\mathord{\\bigwedge}^p A(t,s)\\|\n       \\leq e^{\\sigma(t-s)+a}\n \\qquad(0\\leq s\\leq t\\leq m).$$ There is $C=C(d,K)$ such that $$\\label{eq:exterior-perturbation}\n \\left\\|\\mathord{\\bigwedge}^p\\widehat A(m,0)\n             -\\mathord{\\bigwedge}^p A(m,0)\\right\\|\n \\leq e^{\\sigma m+a}\n       \\left[(1+C\\epsilon e^{a+|\\sigma|})^m-1\\right].$$ For $p=0$ the left side is zero.*\n\n*In particular, use the scales <a href=\"#eq:smooth-scales\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:smooth-scales\">[eq:smooth-scales]</a>, take $m\\leq2n$, $a\\leq C_1bn$, $|\\sigma|\\leq C_2$, and $\\epsilon\\leq C_3e^{-3Bn/2}$, with fixed constants. Then for every fixed $C_0>0$ the left side of <a href=\"#eq:exterior-perturbation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exterior-perturbation\">[eq:exterior-perturbation]</a> is $$\\label{eq:relative-exterior-small}\n o\\!\\left(e^{\\sigma m-C_0bn}\\right),$$ uniformly for all matrices satisfying these hypotheses.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $d\\geq1$ and $K\\geq1$. Let $0\\leq p\\leq d$, let $m\\geq1$, and let $A_0,\\ldots,A_{m-1}$ and $\\widehat A_0,\\ldots,\\widehat A_{m-1}$ be real $d$-by-$d$ matrices such that $$\\|A_t\\|\\leq K,\\qquad\n \\|\\widehat A_t-A_t\\|\\leq\\epsilon\\leq1.$$ Use the same product notation as above for nonnegative indices. Suppose $a\\geq0$, $\\sigma\\in\\mathbb R$, and $$\\|\\mathord{\\bigwedge}^p A(t,s)\\|\n       \\leq e^{\\sigma(t-s)+a}\n \\qquad(0\\leq s\\leq t\\leq m).$$ There is $C=C(d,K)$ such that $$\\label{eq:exterior-perturbation}\n \\left\\|\\mathord{\\bigwedge}^p\\widehat A(m,0)\n             -\\mathord{\\bigwedge}^p A(m,0)\\right\\|\n \\leq e^{\\sigma m+a}\n       \\left[(1+C\\epsilon e^{a+|\\sigma|})^m-1\\right].$$ For $p=0$ the left side is zero.*\n\n*In particular, use the scales <a href=\"#eq:smooth-scales\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:smooth-scales\">[eq:smooth-scales]</a>, take $m\\leq2n$, $a\\leq C_1bn$, $|\\sigma|\\leq C_2$, and $\\epsilon\\leq C_3e^{-3Bn/2}$, with fixed constants. Then for every fixed $C_0>0$ the left side of <a href=\"#eq:exterior-perturbation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exterior-perturbation\">[eq:exterior-perturbation]</a> is $$\\label{eq:relative-exterior-small}\n o\\!\\left(e^{\\sigma m-C_0bn}\\right),$$ uniformly for all matrices satisfying these hypotheses.",
      "explanation": "Expanding a perturbed exterior product by insertion positions yields a relative error estimate. At the prescribed smooth scales this error remains negligible even when the leading volume is small.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-switching",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a compact smooth manifold of positive finite dimension, let $\\nu$ be a smooth positive probability volume in the density sense of [sec:introduction](#sec:introduction), and let $S:M\\to M$ be a smooth diffeomorphism preserving $\\nu$. Fix a Riemannian distance $d_M$ and $\\eta>0$. There exist positive numbers $B_k$ with $\\sum_k B_k^{16}<\\infty$ and a constant $C$ such that the following holds for all sufficiently large $k$, with $n=2^k$.*\n\n\n\n*There is a finite measurable partition $\\mathcal D_k$ of $M$ with $\\#\\mathcal D_k\\le \\exp(CB_kn)$. Define the probability measure $$\\label{eq:pair-law}\n \\rho_k=\\sum_{\\substack{Q\\in\\mathcal D_k\\\\\\nu(Q)>0}}\n       \\frac{(\\nu|_Q)\\otimes(\\nu|_Q)}{\\nu(Q)}.$$ Thus one first samples a cell with its $\\nu$-weight and then samples two independent points in that cell. There are a measurable set $G_k\\subset M\\times M$, with $\\rho_k(G_k)\\ge1-\\eta$, and a measurable map $z_k:G_k\\to M$ such that $$\\label{eq:switch-law}\n (z_k)_*(\\rho_k|_{G_k})\\le 2\\nu$$ and $$\\label{eq:switch-shadow}\n \\sup_{(x,y)\\in G_k}\\left[\n \\max_{0\\le t\\le n}d_M(S^t z_k(x,y),S^t x)\n +\\max_{-n\\le t\\le0}d_M(S^t z_k(x,y),S^t y)\n \\right]\\longrightarrow0.$$ The measure on the left of <a href=\"#eq:switch-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:switch-law\">[eq:switch-law]</a> is not normalized after restriction to $G_k$. The same conclusions hold if $M$ has a smooth boundary.",
        "source_id": "PM-152.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-switching",
          "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a compact smooth manifold of positive finite dimension, let $\\nu$ be a smooth positive probability volume in the density sense of [sec:introduction](#sec:introduction), and let $S:M\\to M$ be a smooth diffeomorphism preserving $\\nu$. Fix a Riemannian distance $d_M$ and $\\eta>0$. There exist positive numbers $B_k$ with $\\sum_k B_k^{16}<\\infty$ and a constant $C$ such that the following holds for all sufficiently large $k$, with $n=2^k$.*\n\n\n\n*There is a finite measurable partition $\\mathcal D_k$ of $M$ with $\\#\\mathcal D_k\\le \\exp(CB_kn)$. Define the probability measure $$\\label{eq:pair-law}\n \\rho_k=\\sum_{\\substack{Q\\in\\mathcal D_k\\\\\\nu(Q)>0}}\n       \\frac{(\\nu|_Q)\\otimes(\\nu|_Q)}{\\nu(Q)}.$$ Thus one first samples a cell with its $\\nu$-weight and then samples two independent points in that cell. There are a measurable set $G_k\\subset M\\times M$, with $\\rho_k(G_k)\\ge1-\\eta$, and a measurable map $z_k:G_k\\to M$ such that $$\\label{eq:switch-law}\n (z_k)_*(\\rho_k|_{G_k})\\le 2\\nu$$ and $$\\label{eq:switch-shadow}\n \\sup_{(x,y)\\in G_k}\\left[\n \\max_{0\\le t\\le n}d_M(S^t z_k(x,y),S^t x)\n +\\max_{-n\\le t\\le0}d_M(S^t z_k(x,y),S^t y)\n \\right]\\longrightarrow0.$$ The measure on the left of <a href=\"#eq:switch-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:switch-law\">[eq:switch-law]</a> is not normalized after restriction to $G_k$. The same conclusions hold if $M$ has a smooth boundary.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $M$ be a compact smooth manifold of positive finite dimension, let $\\nu$ be a smooth positive probability volume in the density sense of [sec:introduction](#sec:introduction), and let $S:M\\to M$ be a smooth diffeomorphism preserving $\\nu$. Fix a Riemannian distance $d_M$ and $\\eta>0$. There exist positive numbers $B_k$ with $\\sum_k B_k^{16}<\\infty$ and a constant $C$ such that the following holds for all sufficiently large $k$, with $n=2^k$.*\n\n\n\n*There is a finite measurable partition $\\mathcal D_k$ of $M$ with $\\#\\mathcal D_k\\le \\exp(CB_kn)$. Define the probability measure $$\\label{eq:pair-law}\n \\rho_k=\\sum_{\\substack{Q\\in\\mathcal D_k\\\\\\nu(Q)>0}}\n       \\frac{(\\nu|_Q)\\otimes(\\nu|_Q)}{\\nu(Q)}.$$ Thus one first samples a cell with its $\\nu$-weight and then samples two independent points in that cell. There are a measurable set $G_k\\subset M\\times M$, with $\\rho_k(G_k)\\ge1-\\eta$, and a measurable map $z_k:G_k\\to M$ such that $$\\label{eq:switch-law}\n (z_k)_*(\\rho_k|_{G_k})\\le 2\\nu$$ and $$\\label{eq:switch-shadow}\n \\sup_{(x,y)\\in G_k}\\left[\n \\max_{0\\le t\\le n}d_M(S^t z_k(x,y),S^t x)\n +\\max_{-n\\le t\\le0}d_M(S^t z_k(x,y),S^t y)\n \\right]\\longrightarrow0.$$ The measure on the left of <a href=\"#eq:switch-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:switch-law\">[eq:switch-law]</a> is not normalized after restriction to $G_k$. The same conclusions hold if $M$ has a smooth boundary.",
      "explanation": "A partition of controlled size permits switching the future and past of most conditionally independent same-cell pairs. The retained output law is dominated by twice the invariant volume without renormalization.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-switch-orbits",
        "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently large $k$ and every $(x,y)\\in G_k$, the first two equations of <a href=\"#eq:four-switch-equations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:four-switch-equations\">[eq:four-switch-equations]</a> have a solution $z$ with $$\\label{eq:sharp-shadow}\n \\max_{0\\le t\\le n}d_M(S^tz,S^tx)\n +\\max_{-n\\le t\\le0}d_M(S^tz,S^ty)\n \\le e^{Cgn}r.$$ In fixed regular coordinates along these halves the solution is unique among displacement arrays of sup norm at most $\\delta$. The complementary solution $w$ has the analogous properties with $x,y$ exchanged. In the product coordinates of the common cell, if $x=(x_s,x_f)$ and $y=(y_s,y_f)$, then $$\\label{eq:ideal-switch}\n z=(y_s,x_f)+o(r),\\qquad w=(x_s,y_f)+o(r),$$ uniformly over all retained pairs. Both outputs remain in the original product cell, and their relevant endpoints remain in the respective four original endpoint bins.",
        "source_id": "PM-152.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-switch-jacobian",
        "exact_quote": "All four derivatives $DH_{xx}(x)$, $DH_{yy}(y)$, $DH_{xy}(z)$, and $DH_{yx}(w)$ are nonsingular. Locally the equations <a href=\"#eq:four-switch-equations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:four-switch-equations\">[eq:four-switch-equations]</a> define a smooth pair map $\\Phi(x,y)=(z,w)$ with $$\\label{eq:pair-jacobian}\n J_{\\nu\\otimes\\nu}\\Phi(x,y)=1+o(1),$$ uniformly on the retained pairs. The conclusion includes empty slow or fast groups.",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-word-frequency",
        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"prop:words\" label=\"prop:words\"></span> Choose $s_1$ so that $K_1\\ge256$, and define the parameters by <a href=\"#eq:word-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:word-parameters\">[eq:word-parameters]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:word-recursion\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:word-recursion\">[eq:word-recursion]</a>. There are families $\\mathcal W_i\\subset\\mathcal A^{m_i}$ of cardinality $L_i$, with $\\mathcal W_1=\\mathcal A$, such that every $w\\in\\mathcal W_{i+1}$ is a concatenation of $N_i$ members of $\\mathcal W_i$ and the following properties hold.*\n\n1.  *<span id=\"word:graph\" label=\"word:graph\"></span>**Relations within groups.** Write $c_i(w)=(u_0,\\ldots,u_{N_i-1})$. For every $0\\le b<N_i/H_i$, every $0\\le k\\le\\lfloor K_i/8\\rfloor$, and every $0\\le r<r+2^k<H_i$, $$[u_{bH_i+r},u_{bH_i+r+2^k}]_i=0.$$*\n\n2.  *<span id=\"word:distance\" label=\"word:distance\"></span>**Distance between words and shifted words.** If $v,w\\in\\mathcal W_{i+1}$ are distinct, then $$d_{\\mathrm H}\\bigl(c_i(v),c_i(w)\\bigr)\n         \\ge(1-\\varepsilon_i)N_i.$$ For all $v,w,z\\in\\mathcal W_{i+1}$, with repetitions allowed, and all $1\\le t<N_i$, $$d_{\\mathrm H}\\bigl((c_i(v)c_i(w))[t,t+N_i),c_i(z)\\bigr)\n         \\ge(1-\\varepsilon_i)N_i.$$*\n\n\n3.  *<span id=\"word:window\" label=\"word:window\"></span>**Distributions of short windows.** Let $U$ be uniform on $\\mathcal W_{i+1}$. For each $0\\le t\\le N_i-D_i^*$ there is a probability $Q_{i,t}$ on $\\mathcal W_i^{D_i^*}$ such that $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{TV}}\\bigl(\\mathcal L(c_i(U)[t,t+D_i^*)),Q_{i,t}\\bigr)\n         \\le\\vartheta.$$ Moreover, for $1\\le d'\\le D_i^*$, any distinct positions $0\\le r_1<\\cdots<r_{d'}<D_i^*$, and any prescribed labels $v_1,\\ldots,v_{d'}\\in\\mathcal W_i$, $$\\label{eq:word-sublist}\n      Q_{i,t}\\{u:u_{r_j}=v_j\\text{ for }1\\le j\\le d'\\}\n           \\le 2\\left(\\frac{2^{2K_i}}{L_i}\\right)^{d'}.$$*\n\n4.  ***Approximation in each individual word.** Suppose $i\\ge2$. For every fixed $w\\in\\mathcal W_{i+1}$, write $c_i(w)=(u_0,\\ldots,u_{N_i-1})$ and let $J$ be uniform on $\\{0,\\ldots,N_i-1\\}$. If $U$ is uniform on $\\mathcal W_i$, then for each $0\\le t\\le N_{i-1}-D_{i-1}^*$, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{TV}}\\left(\n       \\mathcal L\\bigl(c_{i-1}(u_J)[t,t+D_{i-1}^*)\\bigr),\n       \\mathcal L\\bigl(c_{i-1}(U)[t,t+D_{i-1}^*)\\bigr)\n     \\right)\\le\\vartheta.$$ The first law averages the constituents of the single fixed word $w$; it does not average over $\\mathcal W_{i+1}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-152.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-recognition",
        "exact_quote": "For the families in [prop:words](#prop:words), put $$d_i=\\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}(1-\\varepsilon_j),\\qquad\n d_*=\\prod_{j=1}^{\\infty}(1-\\varepsilon_j)>0.$$ Distinct $v,w\\in\\mathcal W_i$ satisfy $$d_{\\mathrm H}(v,w)\\ge d_i m_i\\ge d_*m_i.$$ For all $v,w,z\\in\\mathcal W_i$ and every $1\\le t<m_i$, $$d_{\\mathrm H}\\bigl((vw)[t,t+m_i),z\\bigr)\n     \\ge d_i m_i\\ge d_*m_i.$$ These distances count symbols in $\\mathcal A$, and repeated source words are allowed.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rates",
        "exact_quote": "Let $R_i=\\ell_i/m_i$.         "source_id": "PM-152.1"

        "exact_quote": "The law of <a href=\"#eq:aligned-list\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:aligned-list\">[eq:aligned-list]</a> under $\\mu$ is the law under $\\mu_i$ of the first $D-1$ level-$i$ words beginning at zero.       "exact_statement": "For all sufficiently large dyadic times in the smooth switching estimate, the retained pairs for which $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\#\\{0\\le t<n:f(S^tz)\\ne f(S^tx)\\}\n       &<d_*n/100,\\label{eq:future-symbol-match}\\\\\n \\#\\{-n\\le t<0:f(S^tz)\\ne f(S^ty)\\}\n       &<d_*n/100\\label{eq:past-symbol-match}\n\\end{aligned}$$ have original $\\rho$-mass at least $.96$.",
      "explanation": "Separated compact cores of the fixed symbol partition convert uniform orbit shadowing into symbol agreement on most retained pairs. Output-law domination controls visits outside the cores.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Smooth positive-volume model** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Here a *smooth positive-volume model* of an invertible probability-preserving transformation $(X,\mu,T)$ consists of a compact smooth manifold $M$ of finite dimension, a smooth positive probability volume $\nu$, and a $C^\infty$ diffeomorphism $S$ preserving $\nu$, together with a measure-preserving isomorphism $$F:X_0\longrightarrow M_0,\qquad F\circ T=S\circ F,$$ on invariant conull measurable sets.
- **Smooth positive volume** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Here smooth positive volume means a probability measure with strictly positive smooth local densities, $d\nu=f(x)\|dx_1\cdots dx_d\|$, where $f>0$ is smooth.
- **Standard nonatomic probability space** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A standard nonatomic probability space means a probability space isomorphic, modulo null sets, to the unit interval with Lebesgue probability.
- **Shannon entropy** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a finite probability vector $p$, its Shannon entropy is $$H(p)=-\sum_a p(a)\log_2 p(a),\qquad 0\log_2 0=0.$$
- **Kolmogorov–Sinai entropy** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The Kolmogorov–Sinai entropy of $T$ is $$h_\mu(T)=\sup_{\mathcal P}\lim_{n\to\infty} \frac1n H_\mu\!\left(\bigvee_{j=0}^{n-1}T^{-j}\mathcal P\right),$$ where $\mathcal P$ ranges over finite measurable partitions.
- **Total variation distance** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Total variation distance between finite laws $p,q$ is $\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}(p,q)=\tfrac12\sum_a\|p(a)-q(a)\|$.
- **Conditional entropy** ([source](#pm-h-4)): For finite random variables, conditional entropy means $$H(Y\mid Z)=\sum_{z:\Pr(Z=z)>0}\Pr(Z=z)\, H\bigl(\mathcal L(Y\mid Z=z)\bigr).$$
- **Block average and normalized maximal chord deviation** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For $h\geq 0$ define the block average and normalized maximal chord deviation by $$A_h=2^{-h}\sum_{t=0}^{2^h-1}Y_t, \qquad Z_h=2^{-h}\max_{0\leq u\leq 2^h} \left\|\sum_{t=0}^{u-1}Y_t-uA_h\right\|.$$
- **Orthonormal frame bundle** ([source](#pm-h-7)): The orthonormal frame bundle $\mathcal F M$ consists of the linear isometries $e:\mathbb R^d\to T_xM$, with bundle projection $\pi(e)=x$.
- **Intrinsic exterior volume expansion** ([source](#pm-h-9)): If $E$ is a $q$-dimensional subspace and $A$ is injective, let $$J_q(A\|E)=\\|(\mathord{\bigwedge}^q A)\xi\\|,$$ where $\xi$ is either unit simple $q$-vector spanning $E$.
- **Cell-weighted pair law** ([source](#stmt-thm-switching)): Define the probability measure $$\label{eq:pair-law} \rho_k=\sum_{\substack{Q\in\mathcal D_k\\\nu(Q)>0}} \frac{(\nu\|_Q)\otimes(\nu\|_Q)}{\nu(Q)}.$$
- **Constituent list** ([source](#pm-h-18)): Whenever $w$ is a concatenation of $N_i$ members of $\mathcal W_i$, let $$c_i(w)=(u_0,\ldots,u_{N_i-1})\in\mathcal W_i^{N_i}$$ be its list of constituents.
- **Symbol Hamming distance** ([source](#sec:words)): For two words of the same length, $d_{\mathrm H}(u,v)$ denotes the number of coordinates at which they differ.
- **Aligned word list** ([source](#eq:aligned-list)): For an integer $D\ge2$, define the vector $$\label{eq:aligned-list} \mathcal L_{i,D}(x,t)= \bigl(V_i(T^{\,t+r_i(x,t)+jm_i}x)\bigr)_{j=0}^{D-2}.$$
- **Level graph test** ([source](#pm-h-25)): Two level-$i$ labels $u,v\in\mathcal W_i$ pass the graph test when $[u,v]_i=0$, using their fixed vector identification.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-dyadic-square-sum | \sum_{h\geq 1}\\|Z_h\\|_{L^2}^2 | ≤ | $C\,\mathbb EY_0^2$ | The constant is absolute; no independence assumption is imposed. |
| q-good-frame-error-floor | b_k | ≥ | $2^{-k/10}$ | The positive error sequence depends on the fixed system and exceptional-mass tolerance. |
| q-good-base-measure | \nu(\mathcal G_k) | ≥ | $1-\eta/100$ | The estimate holds at each prescribed dyadic scale. |
| q-smooth-partition-cardinality | \#\mathcal D_k | ≤ | $\exp(CB_kn)$ | The constant and summable cost sequence depend on the fixed smooth model and tolerance. |
| q-retained-pair-mass | \rho_k(G_k) | ≥ | $1-\eta$ | Mass is measured in the original pair law. |
| q-switch-output-domination | (z_k)_*(\rho_k\|_{G_k}) | ≤ | $2\nu$ | The restricted law is not normalized; the estimate is a measure inequality. |
| q-orbit-shadow-radius | \max_{0\le t\le n}d_M(S^tz,S^tx)+\max_{-n\le t\le0}d_M(S^tz,S^ty) | ≤ | $e^{Cgn}r$ | The bound is uniform over retained pairs; constants depend on the fixed smooth data. |
| q-pair-volume-jacobian | J_{\nu\otimes\nu}\Phi(x,y) | = | $1+o(1)$ | The asymptotic error is uniform over retained pairs and includes empty rate groups. |
| q-word-window-tv-tolerance | \vartheta | = | $10^{-4}$ | The fixed tolerance applies to short-window distributions and per-parent approximation. |
| q-probability-vector-tv | \mathbb E\,\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}\left(\frac1q\sum_{b=1}^qY_b,p\right) | ≤ | $\frac12\sqrt{\frac Aq}$ | Independence is between vectors; their internal coordinates may be dependent. |
| q-symbol-recognition-distance | d_{\mathrm H}(v,w) | ≥ | $d_*m_i$ | The positive separation constant is fixed by the entire word hierarchy. |
| q-word-rate-contraction | R_{i+1} | ≤ | $\frac{\varepsilon_i}{100}R_i$ | The rates tend to zero across levels. |
| q-word-rate-lower-bound | R_i | ≥ | $R_1\,2^{-(i-1)(i+20)/2}$ | The initial rate is fixed before choosing a hypothetical smooth model. |
| q-aligned-window-entropy | H_\mu(\mathcal L_{i,D}(\,\cdot\,,t)) | ≥ | $.996(D-1)\ell_i$ | The threshold is independent of the window length, observation time, and invariant probability. |
| q-group-boundary-exception | \alpha_{i,D} | = | $\frac{2D-2}{H_i}$ | This is the stated bound on exceptional probability, rather than an asserted exact failure probability. |
| q-independent-passing-ceiling | Averaged probability of passing the graph test | ≤ | $.62$ | Entropy is averaged over the same index used in the passing probability. |
| q-original-law-passing-upper | \mathbb E_\rho Z | ≤ | $.62$ | Conditioning on the cell has negligible entropy cost. |
| q-original-law-passing-lower | \mathbb E_\rho Z | ≥ | $.912$ | Symbol agreement, phase recovery, and output-law domination are used without normalizing the retained law. |

          "exact_quote": "For each $\\eta>0$ there is a positive sequence $(b_k)_{k\\geq1}$ with $$\\label{eq:b-summability}\n b_k\\geq 2^{-k/10},\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{k\\geq1}b_k^2<\\infty,$$ and compact sets $\\widetilde{\\mathcal G}_k\\subset\\mathcal F M$ and $\\mathcal G_k=\\pi(\\widetilde{\\mathcal G}_k)\\subset M$ such that $$\\widetilde\\nu(\\widetilde{\\mathcal G}_k)\\geq1-\\eta/100,\n \\qquad \\nu(\\mathcal G_k)\\geq1-\\eta/100.$$ For $n=2^k$, $b=b_k$, and $e\\in\\widetilde{\\mathcal G}_k$, define $$A_t=R(\\widetilde S^t e)\\quad(-n\\leq t<n),\n \\qquad\n \\lambda_j=\\frac1{2n}\\sum_{t=-n}^{n-1}\\ln(A_t)_{jj}.$$ Then for every $j$ and every integer $-n\\leq u\\leq n$, $$\\label{eq:good-chord}\n \\left|\\sum_{t=-n}^{u-1}\\ln(A_t)_{jj}\n          -(u+n)\\lambda_j\\right|\\leq bn.$$ Consequently, on every subinterval $-n\\leq u\\leq v\\leq n$, $$\\label{eq:good-subinterval}\n \\left|\\sum_{t=u}^{v-1}\\ln(A_t)_{jj}\n          -(v-u)\\lambda_j\\right|\\leq2bn.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "id": "q-good-base-measure",

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-switching",
          "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a compact smooth manifold of positive finite dimension, let $\\nu$ be a smooth positive probability volume in the density sense of [sec:introduction](#sec:introduction), and let $S:M\\to M$ be a smooth diffeomorphism preserving $\\nu$. Fix a Riemannian distance $d_M$ and $\\eta>0$. There exist positive numbers $B_k$ with $\\sum_k B_k^{16}<\\infty$ and a constant $C$ such that the following holds for all sufficiently large $k$, with $n=2^k$.*\n\n\n\n*There is a finite measurable partition $\\mathcal D_k$ of $M$ with $\\#\\mathcal D_k\\le \\exp(CB_kn)$. Define the probability measure $$\\label{eq:pair-law}\n \\rho_k=\\sum_{\\substack{Q\\in\\mathcal D_k\\\\\\nu(Q)>0}}\n       \\frac{(\\nu|_Q)\\otimes(\\nu|_Q)}{\\nu(Q)}.$$ Thus one first samples a cell with its $\\nu$-weight and then samples two independent points in that cell. There are a measurable set $G_k\\subset M\\times M$, with $\\rho_k(G_k)\\ge1-\\eta$, and a measurable map $z_k:G_k\\to M$ such that $$\\label{eq:switch-law}\n (z_k)_*(\\rho_k|_{G_k})\\le 2\\nu$$ and $$\\label{eq:switch-shadow}\n \\sup_{(x,y)\\in G_k}\\left[\n \\max_{0\\le t\\le n}d_M(S^t z_k(x,y),S^t x)\n +\\max_{-n\\le t\\le0}d_M(S^t z_k(x,y),S^t y)\n \\right]\\longrightarrow0.$$ The measure on the left of <a href=\"#eq:switch-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:switch-law\">[eq:switch-law]</a> is not normalized after restriction to $G_k$. The same conclusions hold if $M$ has a smooth boundary.",

        }

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-dyadic-chords** [claim](#stmt-lem-dyadic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-dyadic): Use the variance identity for adjacent halves, refine cumulative-sum polygons dyadically, and apply a summable convolution estimate. -> Square-summable normalized maximal chord deviations.
- **step-good-frame-paths** [claim](#stmt-prop-good-frames) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-good-frames): Apply stationary chord estimates to bounded diagonal derivative logs and use Markov's inequality to obtain compact good-frame sets. -> Large compact sets with a square-summable chord-error sequence and all-subinterval control.
- **step-finite-splitting** [claim](#stmt-prop-finite-splitting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-finite-splitting): Normalize diagonal sums, estimate triangular products, remove cross-group entries with bounded finite-interval shears, and compute exterior volumes in the resulting oblique bases. -> Invariant slow and fast spaces with product, projection, volume, and mixed-factor bounds.
- **step-bin-space-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-bin-spaces) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bin-spaces): Compare short products in common charts, choose a threshold avoiding representative rates, and compare every witness space with a fixed singular subspace. -> Common thresholds, dimensions, and uniformly invertible endpoint projections.
- **step-relative-exterior-products** [claim](#stmt-lem-relative-exterior) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-relative-exterior): Expand by the positions of perturbed exterior factors and sum the insertion bounds with the binomial formula. -> Relative exterior-product error negligible compared with the leading volume scales.
- **step-orbit-boundary-contraction** [claim](#stmt-lem-switch-orbits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-switch-orbits): Solve a weighted linear boundary problem, remove the weights, and apply a nonlinear contraction in a larger uniqueness tube. Compare the solution with ideal coordinate exchange. -> Complementary genuine orbit joins with sharp shadowing and preserved cell and endpoint labels.
- **step-joint-jacobian-cancellation** [claim](#stmt-lem-switch-jacobian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-switch-jacobian): Identify dominant future and past minors, transfer them relatively to switched orbits, and cancel their products in the exact pair determinant identity. -> Nonsingular endpoint maps and a pair volume Jacobian tending uniformly to unity.
- **step-global-switch-injectivity** [claim](#stmt-lem-switch-injective) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-switch-injective): Cover retained inputs by countably many implicit-function branches. Recover all bin labels from the outputs and apply boundary uniqueness to competing inputs. -> A measurable injective pair switch with countably many smooth change-of-variables pieces.
- **step-smooth-output-law** [claim](#stmt-thm-switching) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-switch-injective-2): Count full product cells, estimate omitted mass using the original marginals, and apply change of variables to the injective complementary pair switch before taking a marginal. -> The smooth switching theorem with a summable partition cost and unnormalized output-law domination.
- **step-vector-average-estimate** [claim](#stmt-lem-word-tv) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-word-tv): Sum coordinate variances using independence between vectors, then apply the second-moment inequality and Cauchy–Schwarz. -> An expected total variation bound for empirical vector averages.
- **step-select-word-families** [claim](#stmt-word-frequency) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-word-tv-2): Sample sequential orthogonal labels inside independent groups, condition on every-parent approximation, and select distinct words satisfying window and shifted-distance bounds by positive-probability estimates. -> Finite word families satisfying every required combinatorial and distributional property.
- **step-word-recognition** [claim](#stmt-lem-recognition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-recognition): Induct on levels, treating offsets divisible by the constituent length separately from offsets crossing constituent boundaries. -> Uniform symbol separation of distinct words and every nontrivial splice.
- **step-word-rate-growth** [claim](#stmt-lem-rates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rates): Divide the recursion by word length, iterate its lower bound, and compare the resulting rate decay with growth of the scale parameters. -> Dyadic lengths, vanishing entropy rates, and divergence of the weighted rate budget.
- **step-invariant-phase-system** [claim](#stmt-prop-system) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-system): Use compactness for invariant probabilities and nested compact faces for an ergodic choice. Uniform phases exclude atoms; finite-coordinate name counts and measurable partition approximation prove zero entropy. -> A standard nonatomic ergodic invertible zero-entropy probability system.
- **step-aligned-sampling-law** [claim](#stmt-lem-aligned-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-aligned-law): Translate each phase class to a boundary and use invariance to equate the masses of parent slots. -> The aligned-list boundary law and uniform parent slots independent of parent labels.
- **step-window-entropy** [claim](#stmt-prop-window-entropy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-window-entropy): Condition on a parent and internal offset, combine the per-parent and uniform-family total variation comparisons, and turn the comparison-law atom bound into an entropy lower bound. -> Uniform nearly maximal entropy of aligned short lists.
- **step-legal-graph-windows** [claim](#stmt-lem-graph-windows) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-graph-windows): Locate the common aligned advance and count uniform group residues whose combined window span crosses a boundary. -> Simultaneous graph relations outside a uniformly vanishing boundary exception.
- **step-select-cheap-tests** [claim](#stmt-lem-cheap-scales) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cheap-scales): Use disjoint dyadic test ranges and contradict cost summability if the relative cost remains bounded away from zero. -> Late tests with negligible partition-conditioning cost relative to aligned-list entropy.
- **step-independent-graph-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-graph-independent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-graph-independent): Bound average heavy mass by entropy, estimate the character matrix using nondegeneracy, and control the light product by its Euclidean norms. -> An averaged graph-passing ceiling for independent inputs.
- **step-symbol-core-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-symbol-matching) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-symbol-matching): Choose separated compact cores of the fixed finite symbol partition, use shadowing for equal-symbol comparisons, and apply output-law domination and Markov's inequality. -> Large original pair mass with few symbol mismatches on both orbit halves.
- **step-realization-contradiction** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-symbol-matching-2): At cheap tests derive the independent passing ceiling using conditional entry entropy. Recover phases and most labels by symbol recognition, transfer legal graph relations through the dominated switch, and obtain an incompatible lower bound in the same original pair law. -> The fixed symbolic system has no smooth positive-volume model in any finite dimension.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-full-character-orthogonality**: The character matrix on the full vector space has mutually orthogonal rows, and every row has squared norm equal to the full vector-space cardinality.
- **invariant-nonzero-orthogonality-counts**: For a fixed nonzero vector, exactly half of the full vector space is orthogonal to it. Removing the zero vector removes an orthogonal partner. Self-pairs remain included.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-check-as-realization-proof**: Treat agreement on finite character matrices as a certificate of the smooth realization obstruction. -> Finite character calculations do not construct the invariant ergodic probability or establish the asymptotic switching contradiction.
- **anti-singular-or-nonsmooth-volume**: Extend the obstruction to smooth maps preserving a singular measure or a nonsmooth density equivalent to volume. -> The controlled unnormalized output law used to transfer measurable symbol observations is not established in those regimes.
- **anti-drop-character-zero-vector**: Use the full character orthogonality identity after deleting the zero column. -> The removed column contributes to every row inner product, so the restricted matrix no longer has the same exact orthogonality identity.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. This checks the finite character-matrix construction in the proof of the independent graph estimate, together with its nonzero orthogonality counts. The source states: “Every row has squared norm $L+1$.” The executable scope is this finite algebraic component; it does not certify the existence of the infinite symbolic system or the main smooth realization obstruction.

```python
# INPUTS: One JSON object containing the integer s.
# PRECONDITIONS: s is one of 1, 2, 3; labels enumerate the full binary vector space.
# OUTPUTS: JSON containing the full character Gram matrix and graph row counts on nonzero vectors.
# TIME: Finite exact character construction and dense matrix multiplication on the declared input domain.
# SPACE: Dense storage for the character matrix, its Gram matrix, and graph counts.
# ARITHMETIC: Binary parity and exact integer matrices using python-flint.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an exception and exits unsuccessfully.
import json
import sys
from flint import fmpz_mat

def run(payload):
    if type(payload) is not dict or set(payload) != {'s'}:
        raise ValueError('expected only s')
    s = payload['s']
    if type(s) is not int or s not in (1, 2, 3):
        raise ValueError('unsupported s')
    size = 1 << (2 * s)
    mask = (1 << s) - 1
    def pairing(u, v):
        left = (u & mask) & (v >> s)
        right = (u >> s) & (v & mask)
        return (left.bit_count() + right.bit_count()) & 1
    characters = fmpz_mat([[1 if pairing(u, v) == 0 else -1 for v in range(size)] for u in range(size)])
    gram = characters * characters.transpose()
    graph_counts = [sum((int(characters[u, v]) + 1) // 2 for v in range(1, size)) for u in range(1, size)]
    return {'s': s, 'gram': [[int(gram[u, v]) for v in range(size)] for u in range(size)], 'graph_counts': graph_counts}

if __name__ == '__main__':
    print(json.dumps(run(json.load(sys.stdin)), separators=(',', ':')))
```

| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A finite candidate word family can be checked against the stated concatenation, graph, distance, window-distribution, and individual-parent requirements. This supplies a validation contract for finite families, without certifying the infinite dynamical obstruction. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The obstruction concerns exact measurable realization with a smooth positive invariant density. It supplies neither a game simulation method nor an impossibility result for finite trajectory approximations. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The recognition inequalities support a finite diagnostic that compares a proposed legal word with other legal words and with nontrivial splices. The diagnostic checks symbol separation and alignment separation for a supplied finite family. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite word proof supplies a sequential randomized construction of candidate constituent lists. Groups are sampled independently, while each new nonzero vector is chosen uniformly from the common kernel of the required earlier-label constraints. Subsequent filtering and family selection require the additional checks proved in the paper. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Equal-length distinct child words give an unambiguous constituent-list representation of each parent. Fixed bijections between words and vector labels allow the same finite representation to support concatenation checks and graph tests. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite-interval matrix estimates control derivative growth and exterior volumes mathematically. They provide no accelerator mapping, memory layout, parallel schedule, or hardware performance contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The alternating-form graph estimate bounds an orthogonality-test probability under averaged entropy assumptions. It does not establish secrecy, computational hardness, or an adversarial security guarantee. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proof excludes an exact smooth positive-volume model for the constructed system. It gives no learning algorithm or finite-data criterion for deciding whether an observed process has such a model. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The selection of cheap scales uses summability and divergence to obtain an asymptotic subsequence. It supplies neither a finite optimisation objective nor a stopping criterion for locating sufficiently late tests from partial sequence information. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The uniform parent-slot law is an identity inside an invariant symbolic process. Its independence assertion concerns a slot and its containing parent, rather than communicating participants or replicated state. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The central ergodic-theory construction selects an invariant probability through compactness and an extreme-point argument. That mathematical existence result does not provide a finite executable representation of the resulting probability space and transformation. |

For each $0\\le t\\le N_i-D_i^*$ there is a probability $Q_{i,t}$ on $\\mathcal W_i^{D_i^*}$ such that $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{TV}}\\bigl(\\mathcal L(c_i(U)[t,t+D_i^*)),Q_{i,t}\\bigr)\n         \\le\\vartheta.$$ Moreover, for $1\\le d'\\le D_i^*$, any distinct positions $0\\le r_1<\\cdots<r_{d'}<D_i^*$, and any prescribed labels $v_1,\\ldots,v_{d'}\\in\\mathcal W_i$, $$\\label{eq:word-sublist}\n      Q_{i,t}\\{u:u_{r_j}=v_j\\text{ for }1\\le j\\le d'\\}\n           \\le 2\\left(\\frac{2^{2K_i}}{L_i}\\right)^{d'}.$$*\n\n4.  ***Approximation in each individual word.** Suppose $i\\ge2$. For every fixed $w\\in\\mathcal W_{i+1}$, write $c_i(w)=(u_0,\\ldots,u_{N_i-1})$ and let $J$ be uniform on $\\{0,\\ldots,N_i-1\\}$. If $U$ is uniform on $\\mathcal W_i$, then for each $0\\le t\\le N_{i-1}-D_{i-1}^*$, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{TV}}\\left(\n       \\mathcal L\\bigl(c_{i-1}(u_J)[t,t+D_{i-1}^*)\\bigr),\n       \\mathcal L\\bigl(c_{i-1}(U)[t,t+D_{i-1}^*)\\bigr)\n     \\right)\\le\\vartheta.$$ The first law averages the constituents of the single fixed word $w$; it does not average over $\\mathcal W_{i+1}$.",
          "source_object": "stmt-word-frequency"
        }
      ],

Suppose that $A_t$, $-n\\leq t<n$, are upper triangular with positive diagonal and $$\\|A_t\\|,\\ \\|A_t^{-1}\\|\\leq A_*,\n \\qquad\n \\left|\\sum_{t=-n}^{u-1}\\ln(A_t)_{jj}\n                  -(u+n)\\lambda_j\\right|\\leq bn$$ for $-n\\leq u\\leq n$ and $1\\leq j\\leq d$, where $\\lambda_j=(2n)^{-1}\\sum_{t=-n}^{n-1}\\ln(A_t)_{jj}$.*\n\n\n*First, order the rates as $\\lambda_{(1)}\\geq\\cdots\\geq\\lambda_{(d)}$. Let $F$ be the set of indices in the second group, let $p=|F|$, and put $$\\Lambda=\\sum_{j\\in F}\\lambda_j,\\qquad\n \\Omega=-\\sum_{j\\notin F}\\lambda_j.$$ There are decompositions $\\mathbb R^d=E_t^s\\oplus E_t^f$, $-n\\leq t\\leq n$, invariant under each $A_t$, with $\\dim E_t^f=p$, whose two projections have norm at most $e^{Cbn}$. For $s\\leq t$ and $l=t-s$ they satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|A(t,s)|E_s^s\\|&\\leq e^{(\\gamma-g)l+Cbn},\n &\n \\|A(s,t)|E_t^f\\|&\\leq e^{-(\\gamma+g)l+Cbn},\n \\label{eq:split-propagation}\\\\\n \\|\\mathord{\\bigwedge}^p A(t,s)\\|&\\leq e^{\\Lambda l+Cbn},\n &\n e^{\\Lambda l-Cbn}\\leq\n J_p(A(t,s)|E_s^f)&\\leq e^{\\Lambda l+Cbn},\n \\label{eq:fast-volume}\\\\\n \\|\\mathord{\\bigwedge}^{d-p} A(s,t)\\|&\\leq e^{\\Omega l+Cbn},\n &\n e^{\\Omega l-Cbn}\\leq\n J_{d-p}(A(s,t)|E_t^s)&\\leq e^{\\Omega l+Cbn}.\n \\label{eq:slow-backward-volume}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If $0<p<d$ and at least one of $v_1,\\ldots,v_p$ belongs to $E_s^s$, then $$\\label{eq:mixed-exterior}\n \\|A(t,s)v_1\\wedge\\cdots\\wedge A(t,s)v_p\\|\n \\leq e^{(\\Lambda-2g)l+Cbn}\\prod_{j=1}^p\\|v_j\\|.$$ The backward analogue has exterior degree $d-p$, exponent $(\\Omega-2g)l+Cbn$, and at least one factor in $E_t^f$. When a group is empty its space is $\\{0\\}$, all statements on that space are vacuous, and the exterior-degree-zero conventions apply.",

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-specific-existential-counterexample** (counterexample_scope): The obstruction concerns the constructed ergodic invertible zero-entropy system; it does not exclude smooth positive-volume models for every zero-entropy system. Blocked use: ['Rejecting a proposed smooth model solely because its abstract system has zero entropy.', 'Treating the theorem as a decision procedure for arbitrary measure-preserving systems.']
- **limit-strictly-positive-smooth-density** (hypothesis): The invariant measure must have a strictly positive smooth density. A singular measure or a nonsmooth density equivalent to volume is outside the asserted obstruction. Blocked use: ['Extending the obstruction to every invariant measure equivalent to volume.', 'Excluding smooth models merely because their invariant probability has full support.']
- **limit-compact-smooth-realization-class** (formal_scope): The excluded models are smooth diffeomorphisms of compact finite-dimensional manifolds. The theorem does not establish the corresponding obstruction for noncompact spaces, infinite-dimensional spaces, or homeomorphisms. Blocked use: ['Claiming nonexistence of all continuous realizations.', 'Applying the result to an arbitrary noninvertible map.']
- **limit-boundary-and-orientation-included** (edge_case): Smooth boundary and nonorientability do not evade the obstruction. The volume hypothesis uses densities rather than a choice of orientation. Blocked use: ['Proposing nonorientability as an exception to the theorem.', 'Treating a compact manifold with smooth boundary as outside the excluded model class.']
- **limit-stationarity-and-second-moment** (hypothesis): The dyadic chord estimate assumes stationarity and a finite second moment. Independence is unnecessary, but these assumptions cannot be replaced by an arbitrary observed time series. Blocked use: ['Inferring square-summability for a nonstationary process.', 'Applying the estimate without verifying the second-moment hypothesis.']
- **limit-scale-wise-good-witnesses** (hypothesis): The good sets are supplied separately at each scale. The construction does not assert eventual goodness at every scale for each individual point or coherent frame witnesses across scales. Blocked use: ['Replacing scale-wise measure estimates by an eventual pointwise assertion.', 'Assuming a continuous or coherent choice of good frames.']
- **limit-triangular-product-preconditions** (hypothesis): Finite-interval splitting requires positive-diagonal upper triangular matrices, uniform matrix and inverse bounds, diagonal chord control, and the displayed logarithmic size condition. The invariant groups additionally require a separating rate threshold. Blocked use: ['Applying the splitting estimates to unrestricted matrix products.', 'Claiming fast and slow groups without checking rate separation.', 'Charging the endpoint change-of-basis loss independently at every time step.']
- **limit-bin-comparison-locality** (hypothesis): Common spaces and endpoint projections are justified for good witnesses in the specified small bins at sufficiently large scales. Endpoint comparisons also require the corresponding endpoints to occupy a common bin. Blocked use: ['Comparing endpoint spaces using only closeness of starting points.', "Applying a bin's fixed projections to paths with different bin labels."]
- **limit-relative-exterior-subinterval-control** (hypothesis): Relative exterior stability uses bounds on every unperturbed subinterval and uniformly small one-step perturbations. An absolute continuity estimate alone does not control an exponentially small leading volume. Blocked use: ['Inferring relative minor stability from a small absolute matrix-product error.', 'Using only an estimate for the full unperturbed product while omitting intermediate subintervals.']
- **limit-retained-pairs-and-local-uniqueness** (hypothesis): The nonlinear joins and their uniqueness apply to retained pairs, fixed endpoint equations, and the prescribed displacement tube. Their wall margins preserve the cell and endpoint labels needed later. Blocked use: ['Extending the switch to arbitrary point pairs.', 'Inferring unrestricted global uniqueness from contraction in the displacement tube.', 'Discarding the cell-wall and endpoint-wall exclusions.']
- **limit-joint-jacobian-needs-injectivity** (hypothesis): The measure estimate requires the complementary pair switch, its joint Jacobian control, and global injectivity. Local nonsingularity alone does not exclude multiplicity when changing variables. Blocked use: ['Deriving the output measure bound from the Jacobian of a single join.', 'Summing local image densities without proving disjointness of the images.']
- **limit-original-unnormalized-pair-law** (formal_scope): The switch domination concerns restriction of the original cell-weighted pair law without renormalization. The final upper and lower estimates must use that same original law. Blocked use: ['Normalizing the retained law while retaining the stated domination factor.', 'Comparing an upper bound in the original experiment with a lower bound conditioned on switch success.']
- **limit-word-family-selection-contract** (hypothesis): The finite word construction uses the prescribed initial size and recursion and requires all graph, distance, window, and individual-parent conditions together. Enforcing orthogonality alone does not produce the required families. Blocked use: ['Substituting arbitrary orthogonal label lists for the selected word families.', 'Omitting shifted comparisons or individual-parent approximation.', 'Reading positive-probability existence as a practical runtime bound.']
- **limit-filtering-changes-sampling-law** (hypothesis): Independence between groups and uniform individual-label marginals belong to the unconditioned sampling construction. They are not asserted after conditioning on the per-parent approximation event. Blocked use: ['Reusing unconditioned independence after filtering.', 'Assuming that the selected family has uniform constituent frequencies.']
- **limit-infinite-system-not-finite-certificate** (nonconstructive): The full phase extension and its ergodic probability use compactness and selection through nested compact faces. A finite family check does not construct or certify the entire invariant system. Blocked use: ['Claiming that a finite word sample certifies the main nonrealization theorem.', 'Treating the existence proof as a finite sampler for the selected ergodic probability.']
- **limit-boundary-law-not-uniform-word-law** (formal_scope): The boundary-conditioned probability gives a uniform parent-slot index independent of its containing parent. It does not assert uniform frequencies of the word labels themselves. Blocked use: ['Replacing the invariant word distribution by the uniform family distribution.', 'Confusing slot-parent independence with independence of adjacent word labels.']
- **limit-window-and-lag-regimes** (hypothesis): The entropy and simultaneous graph-window bounds apply at sufficiently large levels and to the stated power-of-two window lengths. Graph failures from crossing group boundaries remain part of the estimate. Blocked use: ['Applying the graph guarantee to arbitrary lags.', 'Treating all corresponding entries as unconditionally orthogonal.', 'Using the eventual entropy coefficient at an unchecked early level.']
- **limit-cheap-scales-no-uniform-horizon** (nonconstructive): Cheap tests are selected asymptotically from summability of the smooth costs and divergence of the symbolic rate budget. The argument supplies no finite search horizon for an arbitrary admissible cost sequence. Blocked use: ['Promising a stopping time for finding a sufficiently cheap scale from a finite prefix.', 'Using an arbitrary dyadic time in place of the selected subsequence.']
- **limit-conditional-independence-full-index** (hypothesis): The independent graph bound assumes independent samples from the indexed laws and averaged entropy bounds over the same index. In its application the full index includes both the sampled cell and the fixed entry. Blocked use: ['Assuming independence after mixing over a shared random entry while conditioning only on the cell.', 'Replacing averaged conditional entropy by an unsupported pointwise entropy bound.', 'Applying the eventual graph ceiling to arbitrary small alphabets.']
- **limit-measurable-symbol-core-transfer** (nonconstructive): Symbol agreement uses compact cores of the fixed measurable symbol partition, uniform geometric shadowing, and output-law domination. The measurable conjugacy supplies no continuity or effective core-selection procedure. Blocked use: ['Transferring geometric proximity directly to equality of arbitrary measurable labels.', 'Replacing the fixed symbol partition by growing label partitions without a new argument.']
- **limit-recognition-requires-symbol-separation** (hypothesis): Recovery of phases and most labels relies on the positive symbol-distance and splice-distance bounds. Constituent-label separation alone does not cover arbitrary symbol offsets. Blocked use: ['Claiming phase recovery for generic concatenated words.', 'Re-pairing entries after selecting a switch instead of retaining the original aligned indices.']
- **limit-finite-character-check-scope** (formal_scope): An exact finite character calculation checks an algebraic component of the independent graph estimate. It does not verify the averaged entropy argument, the asymptotic scale selection, or the main obstruction. Blocked use: ['Reporting a finite character calculation as certification of the entire theorem.', 'Treating agreement in tested dimensions as an exhaustive proof for every dimension.']
