{
  "entries": [
    {
      "blocked_use": [
        "Rejecting a proposed smooth model solely because its abstract system has zero entropy.",
        "Treating the theorem as a decision procedure for arbitrary measure-preserving systems."
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
          "exact_quote": "There exists an ergodic invertible measure-preserving transformation $(X,\\mu,T)$ of a standard nonatomic probability space such that $h_\\mu(T)=0$ and $(X,\\mu,T)$ is not measurably conjugate, on invariant conull sets, to any $C^\\infty$ diffeomorphism of a compact finite-dimensional manifold preserving a strictly positive smooth probability density.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-specific-existential-counterexample",
      "kind": "counterexample_scope",
      "statement": "The obstruction concerns the constructed ergodic invertible zero-entropy system; it does not exclude smooth positive-volume models for every zero-entropy system.",
      "support": "inferred"
    },
    {
      "blocked_use": [
        "Extending the obstruction to every invariant measure equivalent to volume.",
        "Excluding smooth models merely because their invariant probability has full support."
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-auto-remark-1",
          "exact_quote": "The proof makes no analogous assertion for models carrying only a singular measure or a nonsmooth density equivalent to volume.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "sec:introduction",
          "exact_quote": "Full support does not require a smooth positive density.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-strictly-positive-smooth-density",
      "kind": "hypothesis",
      "statement": "The invariant measure must have a strictly positive smooth density. A singular measure or a nonsmooth density equivalent to volume is outside the asserted obstruction.",
      "support": "stated"
    },
    {
      "blocked_use": [
        "Claiming nonexistence of all continuous realizations.",
        "Applying the result to an arbitrary noninvertible map."
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
          "exact_quote": "There exists an ergodic invertible measure-preserving transformation $(X,\\mu,T)$ of a standard nonatomic probability space such that $h_\\mu(T)=0$ and $(X,\\mu,T)$ is not measurably conjugate, on invariant conull sets, to any $C^\\infty$ diffeomorphism of a compact finite-dimensional manifold preserving a strictly positive smooth probability density.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-compact-smooth-realization-class",
      "kind": "formal_scope",
      "statement": "The excluded models are smooth diffeomorphisms of compact finite-dimensional manifolds. The theorem does not establish the corresponding obstruction for noncompact spaces, infinite-dimensional spaces, or homeomorphisms.",
      "support": "inferred"
    },
    {
      "blocked_use": [
        "Proposing nonorientability as an exception to the theorem.",
        "Treating a compact manifold with smooth boundary as outside the excluded model class."
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
          "exact_quote": "The manifold may have smooth boundary and need not be orientable.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "sec:introduction",
          "exact_quote": "Here smooth positive volume means a probability measure with strictly positive smooth local densities, $d\\nu=f(x)|dx_1\\cdots dx_d|$, where $f>0$ is smooth.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-boundary-and-orientation-included",
      "kind": "edge_case",
      "statement": "Smooth boundary and nonorientability do not evade the obstruction. The volume hypothesis uses densities rather than a choice of orientation.",
      "support": "stated"
    },
    {
      "blocked_use": [
        "Inferring square-summability for a nonstationary process.",
        "Applying the estimate without verifying the second-moment hypothesis."
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-6",
          "exact_quote": "#### Deviation from a dyadic chord\n\nLet $(Y_t)_{t\\in\\mathbb Z}$ be a real stationary process with $\\mathbb EY_0^2<\\infty$.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-dyadic",
          "exact_quote": "No independence of the two halves is used.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-stationarity-and-second-moment",
      "kind": "hypothesis",
      "statement": "The dyadic chord estimate assumes stationarity and a finite second moment. Independence is unnecessary, but these assumptions cannot be replaced by an arbitrary observed time series.",
      "support": "stated"
    },
    {
      "blocked_use": [
        "Replacing scale-wise measure estimates by an eventual pointwise assertion.",
        "Assuming a continuous or coherent choice of good frames."
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-good-frames",
          "exact_quote": "No measurable choice of a frame over each point of $\\mathcal G_k$ is needed.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-scale-wise-good-witnesses",
      "kind": "hypothesis",
      "statement": "The good sets are supplied separately at each scale. The construction does not assert eventual goodness at every scale for each individual point or coherent frame witnesses across scales.",
      "support": "inferred"
    },
    {
      "blocked_use": [
        "Applying the splitting estimates to unrestricted matrix products.",
        "Claiming fast and slow groups without checking rate separation.",
        "Charging the endpoint change-of-basis loss independently at every time step."
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-finite-splitting",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $d\\geq1$ and $A_*>1$. There is a constant $C$, depending only on these two quantities, with the following properties. Let $n\\geq2$, $b>0$, and $\\ln(2n+1)\\leq bn$. Suppose that $A_t$, $-n\\leq t<n$, are upper triangular with positive diagonal and $$\\|A_t\\|,\\ \\|A_t^{-1}\\|\\leq A_*,\n \\qquad\n \\left|\\sum_{t=-n}^{u-1}\\ln(A_t)_{jj}\n                  -(u+n)\\lambda_j\\right|\\leq bn$$ for $-n\\leq u\\leq n$ and $1\\leq j\\leq d$, where $\\lambda_j=(2n)^{-1}\\sum_{t=-n}^{n-1}\\ln(A_t)_{jj}$.*\n\n\n*First, order the rates as $\\lambda_{(1)}\\geq\\cdots\\geq\\lambda_{(d)}$. For every $0\\leq q\\leq d$ and every $-n\\leq s\\leq t\\leq n$, $$\\label{eq:exterior-rate-comparison}\n \\left|\\ln\\|\\mathord{\\bigwedge}^q A(t,s)\\|\n       -(t-s)\\sum_{j=1}^q\\lambda_{(j)}\\right|\\leq Cbn.$$ The analogous estimate for $A(s,t)$ uses the $q$ largest members of $\\{-\\lambda_1,\\ldots,-\\lambda_d\\}$.*\n\n\n\n\n\n*Next, suppose that real $\\gamma$ and $g>0$ separate all the rates: each $\\lambda_j$ is at most $\\gamma-g$ or at least $\\gamma+g$. Let $F$ be the set of indices in the second group, let $p=|F|$, and put $$\\Lambda=\\sum_{j\\in F}\\lambda_j,\\qquad\n \\Omega=-\\sum_{j\\notin F}\\lambda_j.$$ There are decompositions $\\mathbb R^d=E_t^s\\oplus E_t^f$, $-n\\leq t\\leq n$, invariant under each $A_t$, with $\\dim E_t^f=p$, whose two projections have norm at most $e^{Cbn}$. For $s\\leq t$ and $l=t-s$ they satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|A(t,s)|E_s^s\\|&\\leq e^{(\\gamma-g)l+Cbn},\n &\n \\|A(s,t)|E_t^f\\|&\\leq e^{-(\\gamma+g)l+Cbn},\n \\label{eq:split-propagation}\\\\\n \\|\\mathord{\\bigwedge}^p A(t,s)\\|&\\leq e^{\\Lambda l+Cbn},\n &\n e^{\\Lambda l-Cbn}\\leq\n J_p(A(t,s)|E_s^f)&\\leq e^{\\Lambda l+Cbn},\n \\label{eq:fast-volume}\\\\\n \\|\\mathord{\\bigwedge}^{d-p} A(s,t)\\|&\\leq e^{\\Omega l+Cbn},\n &\n e^{\\Omega l-Cbn}\\leq\n J_{d-p}(A(s,t)|E_t^s)&\\leq e^{\\Omega l+Cbn}.\n \\label{eq:slow-backward-volume}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If $0<p<d$ and at least one of $v_1,\\ldots,v_p$ belongs to $E_s^s$, then $$\\label{eq:mixed-exterior}\n \\|A(t,s)v_1\\wedge\\cdots\\wedge A(t,s)v_p\\|\n \\leq e^{(\\Lambda-2g)l+Cbn}\\prod_{j=1}^p\\|v_j\\|.$$ The backward analogue has exterior degree $d-p$, exponent $(\\Omega-2g)l+Cbn$, and at least one factor in $E_t^f$. When a group is empty its space is $\\{0\\}$, all statements on that space are vacuous, and the exterior-degree-zero conventions apply.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-triangular-product-preconditions",
      "kind": "hypothesis",
      "statement": "Finite-interval splitting requires positive-diagonal upper triangular matrices, uniform matrix and inverse bounds, diagonal chord control, and the displayed logarithmic size condition. The invariant groups additionally require a separating rate threshold.",
      "support": "stated"
    },
    {
      "blocked_use": [
        "Comparing endpoint spaces using only closeness of starting points.",
        "Applying a bin's fixed projections to paths with different bin labels."
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-bin-spaces",
          "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $k$, each spatial bin $U$ meeting $\\mathcal G_k$ has a number $$\\gamma_U\\in[g,(8d+1)g]$$ such that every good witness frame over $U$ has all its rates at most $\\gamma_U-g$ or at least $\\gamma_U+g$, with the same number $p_U$ in the second group. Apply [prop:finite-splitting](#prop:finite-splitting) using this threshold. There is $c>0$, independent of the bin and the witnesses, such that:*\n\n1.  *The spaces $E_0^s$ from any two good witnesses over $U$ are within $e^{-c gL}$ of one another in its coordinate chart. The same holds for the spaces $E_0^f$.*\n\n2.  *For good paths starting in $U$ whose time-$n$ endpoints lie in a common endpoint bin $V$, their spaces $E_n^f$ are within $e^{-c gL}$ of one another in the chart of $V$. For paths whose time-$(-n)$ endpoints lie in a common bin, the same assertion holds for $E_{-n}^s$.*\n\n*In particular, fix one representative space for each nonempty combination of bins in <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span>. Orthogonal projection onto it, restricted to any corresponding actual endpoint space, is an isomorphism with inverse norm at most $2$. If $p_U=0$ or $p_U=d$, the relevant zero or full spaces agree exactly.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-bin-comparison-locality",
      "kind": "hypothesis",
      "statement": "Common spaces and endpoint projections are justified for good witnesses in the specified small bins at sufficiently large scales. Endpoint comparisons also require the corresponding endpoints to occupy a common bin.",
      "support": "stated"
    },
    {
      "blocked_use": [
        "Inferring relative minor stability from a small absolute matrix-product error.",
        "Using only an estimate for the full unperturbed product while omitting intermediate subintervals."
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-relative-exterior",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $d\\geq1$ and $K\\geq1$. Let $0\\leq p\\leq d$, let $m\\geq1$, and let $A_0,\\ldots,A_{m-1}$ and $\\widehat A_0,\\ldots,\\widehat A_{m-1}$ be real $d$-by-$d$ matrices such that $$\\|A_t\\|\\leq K,\\qquad\n \\|\\widehat A_t-A_t\\|\\leq\\epsilon\\leq1.$$ Use the same product notation as above for nonnegative indices. Suppose $a\\geq0$, $\\sigma\\in\\mathbb R$, and $$\\|\\mathord{\\bigwedge}^p A(t,s)\\|\n       \\leq e^{\\sigma(t-s)+a}\n \\qquad(0\\leq s\\leq t\\leq m).$$ There is $C=C(d,K)$ such that $$\\label{eq:exterior-perturbation}\n \\left\\|\\mathord{\\bigwedge}^p\\widehat A(m,0)\n             -\\mathord{\\bigwedge}^p A(m,0)\\right\\|\n \\leq e^{\\sigma m+a}\n       \\left[(1+C\\epsilon e^{a+|\\sigma|})^m-1\\right].$$ For $p=0$ the left side is zero.*\n\n*In particular, use the scales <a href=\"#eq:smooth-scales\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:smooth-scales\">[eq:smooth-scales]</a>, take $m\\leq2n$, $a\\leq C_1bn$, $|\\sigma|\\leq C_2$, and $\\epsilon\\leq C_3e^{-3Bn/2}$, with fixed constants. Then for every fixed $C_0>0$ the left side of <a href=\"#eq:exterior-perturbation\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:exterior-perturbation\">[eq:exterior-perturbation]</a> is $$\\label{eq:relative-exterior-small}\n o\\!\\left(e^{\\sigma m-C_0bn}\\right),$$ uniformly for all matrices satisfying these hypotheses.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-11",
          "exact_quote": "This must be a relative estimate: a leading volume can be exponentially small, so continuity with an absolute error alone is insufficient.\n\n\n<div id=\"lem:relative-exterior\" class=\"lemma\">\n\n\n**Lemma 6** (Exterior-product perturbation).",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-relative-exterior-subinterval-control",
      "kind": "hypothesis",
      "statement": "Relative exterior stability uses bounds on every unperturbed subinterval and uniformly small one-step perturbations. An absolute continuity estimate alone does not control an exponentially small leading volume.",
      "support": "stated"
    },
    {
      "blocked_use": [
        "Extending the switch to arbitrary point pairs.",
        "Inferring unrestricted global uniqueness from contraction in the displacement tube.",
        "Discarding the cell-wall and endpoint-wall exclusions."
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-switch-orbits",
          "exact_quote": "For every sufficiently large $k$ and every $(x,y)\\in G_k$, the first two equations of <a href=\"#eq:four-switch-equations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:four-switch-equations\">[eq:four-switch-equations]</a> have a solution $z$ with $$\\label{eq:sharp-shadow}\n \\max_{0\\le t\\le n}d_M(S^tz,S^tx)\n +\\max_{-n\\le t\\le0}d_M(S^tz,S^ty)\n \\le e^{Cgn}r.$$ In fixed regular coordinates along these halves the solution is unique among displacement arrays of sup norm at most $\\delta$. The complementary solution $w$ has the analogous properties with $x,y$ exchanged. In the product coordinates of the common cell, if $x=(x_s,x_f)$ and $y=(y_s,y_f)$, then $$\\label{eq:ideal-switch}\n z=(y_s,x_f)+o(r),\\qquad w=(x_s,y_f)+o(r),$$ uniformly over all retained pairs. Both outputs remain in the original product cell, and their relevant endpoints remain in the respective four original endpoint bins.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-retained-pairs-and-local-uniqueness",
      "kind": "hypothesis",
      "statement": "The nonlinear joins and their uniqueness apply to retained pairs, fixed endpoint equations, and the prescribed displacement tube. Their wall margins preserve the cell and endpoint labels needed later.",
      "support": "stated"
    },
    {
      "blocked_use": [
        "Deriving the output measure bound from the Jacobian of a single join.",
        "Summing local image densities without proving disjointness of the images."
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-switch-injective",
          "exact_quote": "The map $\\Phi:G_k\\to M\\times M$, $\\Phi(x,y)=(z,w)$, is measurable and injective. Its restriction to countably many measurable pieces agrees with smooth local diffeomorphisms having the Jacobian in <a href=\"#eq:pair-jacobian\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:pair-jacobian\">[eq:pair-jacobian]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-switch-injective-2",
          "exact_quote": "Their images are disjoint by global injectivity.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-joint-jacobian-needs-injectivity",
      "kind": "hypothesis",
      "statement": "The measure estimate requires the complementary pair switch, its joint Jacobian control, and global injectivity. Local nonsingularity alone does not exclude multiplicity when changing variables.",
      "support": "stated"
    },
    {
      "blocked_use": [
        "Normalizing the retained law while retaining the stated domination factor.",
        "Comparing an upper bound in the original experiment with a lower bound conditioned on switch success."
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-switching",
          "exact_quote": "The measure on the left of <a href=\"#eq:switch-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:switch-law\">[eq:switch-law]</a> is not normalized after restriction to $G_k$.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-switch-injective-2",
          "exact_quote": "Restriction to endpoint-label pieces or to $G_k$ is never followed by normalization, and zero-volume cells contribute nothing.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-original-unnormalized-pair-law",
      "kind": "formal_scope",
      "statement": "The switch domination concerns restriction of the original cell-weighted pair law without renormalization. The final upper and lower estimates must use that same original law.",
      "support": "stated"
    },
    {
      "blocked_use": [
        "Substituting arbitrary orthogonal label lists for the selected word families.",
        "Omitting shifted comparisons or individual-parent approximation.",
        "Reading positive-probability existence as a practical runtime bound."
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-word-frequency",
          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"prop:words\" label=\"prop:words\"></span> Choose $s_1$ so that $K_1\\ge256$, and define the parameters by <a href=\"#eq:word-parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:word-parameters\">[eq:word-parameters]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:word-recursion\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:word-recursion\">[eq:word-recursion]</a>. There are families $\\mathcal W_i\\subset\\mathcal A^{m_i}$ of cardinality $L_i$, with $\\mathcal W_1=\\mathcal A$, such that every $w\\in\\mathcal W_{i+1}$ is a concatenation of $N_i$ members of $\\mathcal W_i$ and the following properties hold.*\n\n1.  *<span id=\"word:graph\" label=\"word:graph\"></span>**Relations within groups.** Write $c_i(w)=(u_0,\\ldots,u_{N_i-1})$. For every $0\\le b<N_i/H_i$, every $0\\le k\\le\\lfloor K_i/8\\rfloor$, and every $0\\le r<r+2^k<H_i$, $$[u_{bH_i+r},u_{bH_i+r+2^k}]_i=0.$$*\n\n2.  *<span id=\"word:distance\" label=\"word:distance\"></span>**Distance between words and shifted words.** If $v,w\\in\\mathcal W_{i+1}$ are distinct, then $$d_{\\mathrm H}\\bigl(c_i(v),c_i(w)\\bigr)\n         \\ge(1-\\varepsilon_i)N_i.$$ For all $v,w,z\\in\\mathcal W_{i+1}$, with repetitions allowed, and all $1\\le t<N_i$, $$d_{\\mathrm H}\\bigl((c_i(v)c_i(w))[t,t+N_i),c_i(z)\\bigr)\n         \\ge(1-\\varepsilon_i)N_i.$$*\n\n\n3.  *<span id=\"word:window\" label=\"word:window\"></span>**Distributions of short windows.** Let $U$ be uniform on $\\mathcal W_{i+1}$. For each $0\\le t\\le N_i-D_i^*$ there is a probability $Q_{i,t}$ on $\\mathcal W_i^{D_i^*}$ such that $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{TV}}\\bigl(\\mathcal L(c_i(U)[t,t+D_i^*)),Q_{i,t}\\bigr)\n         \\le\\vartheta.$$ Moreover, for $1\\le d'\\le D_i^*$, any distinct positions $0\\le r_1<\\cdots<r_{d'}<D_i^*$, and any prescribed labels $v_1,\\ldots,v_{d'}\\in\\mathcal W_i$, $$\\label{eq:word-sublist}\n      Q_{i,t}\\{u:u_{r_j}=v_j\\text{ for }1\\le j\\le d'\\}\n           \\le 2\\left(\\frac{2^{2K_i}}{L_i}\\right)^{d'}.$$*\n\n4.  ***Approximation in each individual word.** Suppose $i\\ge2$. For every fixed $w\\in\\mathcal W_{i+1}$, write $c_i(w)=(u_0,\\ldots,u_{N_i-1})$ and let $J$ be uniform on $\\{0,\\ldots,N_i-1\\}$. If $U$ is uniform on $\\mathcal W_i$, then for each $0\\le t\\le N_{i-1}-D_{i-1}^*$, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{TV}}\\left(\n       \\mathcal L\\bigl(c_{i-1}(u_J)[t,t+D_{i-1}^*)\\bigr),\n       \\mathcal L\\bigl(c_{i-1}(U)[t,t+D_{i-1}^*)\\bigr)\n     \\right)\\le\\vartheta.$$ The first law averages the constituents of the single fixed word $w$; it does not average over $\\mathcal W_{i+1}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-word-tv-2",
          "exact_quote": "The conditioning event thus has positive probability.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-word-family-selection-contract",
      "kind": "hypothesis",
      "statement": "The finite word construction uses the prescribed initial size and recursion and requires all graph, distance, window, and individual-parent conditions together. Enforcing orthogonality alone does not produce the required families.",
      "support": "stated"
    },
    {
      "blocked_use": [
        "Reusing unconditioned independence after filtering.",
        "Assuming that the selected family has uniform constituent frequencies."
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-word-tv-2",
          "exact_quote": "No uniform marginal or independence between groups is asserted for $P_i$.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-filtering-changes-sampling-law",
      "kind": "hypothesis",
      "statement": "Independence between groups and uniform individual-label marginals belong to the unconditioned sampling construction. They are not asserted after conditioning on the per-parent approximation event.",
      "support": "stated"
    },
    {
      "blocked_use": [
        "Claiming that a finite word sample certifies the main nonrealization theorem.",
        "Treating the existence proof as a finite sampler for the selected ergodic probability."
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-22",
          "exact_quote": "Compactness proves that $X$ is nonempty.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-system",
          "exact_quote": "The nested intersection is nonempty and contains only one measure, because the integrals of every $f_j$ are then fixed and determine a probability.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-infinite-system-not-finite-certificate",
      "kind": "nonconstructive",
      "statement": "The full phase extension and its ergodic probability use compactness and selection through nested compact faces. A finite family check does not construct or certify the entire invariant system.",
      "support": "stated"
    },
    {
      "blocked_use": [
        "Replacing the invariant word distribution by the uniform family distribution.",
        "Confusing slot-parent independence with independence of adjacent word labels."
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-23",
          "exact_quote": "It describes a point observed at a level-$i$ boundary; it is not asserted to give uniform word frequencies.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-aligned-law",
          "exact_quote": "The law of <a href=\"#eq:aligned-list\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:aligned-list\">[eq:aligned-list]</a> under $\\mu$ is the law under $\\mu_i$ of the first $D-1$ level-$i$ words beginning at zero. Under $\\mu_i$, the index $J=a_{i+2}/m_i$ is uniform on $\\{0,\\ldots,N_iN_{i+1}-1\\}$ and is independent of $V_{i+2}$. More precisely, for each such $j$ and $v\\in\\mathcal W_{i+2}$, $$\\label{eq:parent-slot-law}\n \\mu_i(J=j,V_{i+2}=v)\n =\\frac{\\mu_{i+2}(V_{i+2}=v)}{N_iN_{i+1}}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-boundary-law-not-uniform-word-law",
      "kind": "formal_scope",
      "statement": "The boundary-conditioned probability gives a uniform parent-slot index independent of its containing parent. It does not assert uniform frequencies of the word labels themselves.",
      "support": "stated"
    },
    {
      "blocked_use": [
        "Applying the graph guarantee to arbitrary lags.",
        "Treating all corresponding entries as unconditionally orthogonal.",
        "Using the eventual entropy coefficient at an unchecked early level."
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-window-entropy",
          "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $i$, for every power of two $2\\le D\\le D_i^*$, and every integer $t$, $$\\label{eq:window-entropy}\n H_\\mu(\\mathcal L_{i,D}(\\,\\cdot\\,,t))\n \\ge .996(D-1)\\ell_i,\\qquad \\ell_i=\\log L_i.$$ The large-$i$ threshold is independent of $D,t$ and of the invariant probability on $X$.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-graph-windows",
          "exact_quote": "Let $2\\le D\\le D_i^*$ be a power of two and set $n=m_iD$. For all sufficiently large $i$, with $\\mu$-probability at least $$1-\\alpha_{i,D},\\qquad\n \\alpha_{i,D}=\\frac{2D-2}{H_i},$$ all corresponding entries of $\\mathcal L_{i,D}(x,-n)$ and $\\mathcal L_{i,D}(x,0)$ pass the level-$i$ graph test simultaneously. In particular $\\sup_{D\\le D_i^*}\\alpha_{i,D}\\to0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-152.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "limit-window-and-lag-regimes",
      "kind": "hypothesis",
      "statement": "The entropy and simultaneous graph-window bounds apply at sufficiently large levels and to the stated power-of-two window lengths. Graph failures from crossing group boundaries remain part of the estimate.",
      "support": "stated"

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 152 (`lean/docs/152.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [A zero-entropy system without a smooth positive-volume model](../../preprints/A-finite-entropy-system-without-a-smooth-positive-volume-model-September-25-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The paper asks whether a measure-preserving system can be represented by a smooth diffeomorphism preserving positive smooth volume. The formalized supporting result constructs an ergodic invertible transformation of a standard nonatomic probability space with finite Kolmogorov–Sinai entropy that has no such model on any compact finite-dimensional manifold, including manifolds with smooth boundary. The comparison is measurable conjugacy after discarding null sets.
>
> The linked statement gives finite entropy; the paper's stronger zero-entropy conclusion is outside this statement.

Comparator source `SmoothObstruction.lean` (4753 bytes, sha256 `096a65243fb33f71fe673f9ccecc7ce8e73faa73b7b3e8a3a4f641838525c47b`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI


open MeasureTheory Set Filter
open scoped ENNReal Topology Manifold ContDiff

namespace SmoothObstruction

/-- Shannon entropy in bits; the value at mass zero is zero. -/
noncomputable def entropyTerm (p : ℝ) : ℝ := -(p * Real.log p) / Real.log 2

/-- The entropy of the length-`n` join of the partition with labels in `Fin q`. -/
noncomputable def blockEntropy {X : Type*} [MeasurableSpace X]
    (μ : Measure X) (T : X → X) {q : ℕ} (P : X → Fin q) (n : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  ∑ w : Fin n → Fin q,
    entropyTerm ((μ {x | ∀ j : Fin n, P ((T^[j.val]) x) = w j}).toReal)

/-- All finite-partition entropy rates exist and have one finite real upper
bound. This spells out `sup_P lim_n H(join_{j<n} T^{-j}P)/n < ∞` without
choosing a value for limits of nonconvergent sequences. Empty atoms are allowed. -/
def FiniteKSEntropy {X : Type*} [MeasurableSpace X]
    (μ : Measure X) (T : X → X) : Prop :=
  ∃ C : ℝ, ∀ (q : ℕ) (P : X → Fin (q + 1)), Measurable P →
    ∃ h : ℝ, h ≤ C ∧
      Tendsto (fun n : ℕ => blockEntropy μ T P n / (n : ℝ)) atTop (𝓝 h)

/-- Measure-space isomorphism after removing measurable null sets. -/
def ConullIsomorphic {X Y : Type*} [MeasurableSpace X] [MeasurableSpace Y]
    (μ : Measure X) (ν : Measure Y) : Prop :=
  ∃ (A : Set X) (B : Set Y), MeasurableSet A ∧ MeasurableSet B ∧
    μ Aᶜ = 0 ∧ ν Bᶜ = 0 ∧
    ∃ e : A ≃ᵐ B,
      MeasurePreserving e (μ.comap (Subtype.val : A → X))
        (ν.comap (Subtype.val : B → Y))

/-- Standard nonatomic in the source's explicit modulo-null-set sense. -/
def StandardNonatomic {X : Type*} [MeasurableSpace X] (μ : Measure X) : Prop :=
  ConullIsomorphic μ
    (volume.comap (Subtype.val : Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) 1 → ℝ))

/-- Measurable conjugacy on invariant conull measurable subsets, not a
continuous conjugacy and not merely a factor map. -/
def ConullConjugate {X Y : Type*} [MeasurableSpace X] [MeasurableSpace Y]
    (μ : Measure X) (T : X → X) (ν : Measure Y) (S : Y → Y) : Prop :=
  ∃ (A : Set X) (B : Set Y), MeasurableSet A ∧ MeasurableSet B ∧
    μ Aᶜ = 0 ∧ ν Bᶜ = 0 ∧ T ⁻¹' A = A ∧ S ⁻¹' B = B ∧
    ∃ e : A ≃ᵐ B,
      MeasurePreserving e (μ.comap (Subtype.val : A → X))
        (ν.comap (Subtype.val : B → Y)) ∧
      ∀ (x y : A), T x = y → (e y : Y) = S (e x)

/-- Positive smooth local coordinate densities. In a half-space chart,
`ContDiffOn` includes smoothness up to the boundary. Absolute Lebesgue density
is used; no orientation or volume form is part of the data. -/
def SmoothPositiveDensity {d : ℕ} {H : Type*} [TopologicalSpace H]
    (I : ModelWithCorners ℝ (EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin d)) H)
    {M : Type*} [TopologicalSpace M] [ChartedSpace H M] [MeasurableSpace M]
    (ν : Measure M) : Prop :=
  ∀ x : M, ∃ f : EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin d) → ℝ,
    ContDiffOn ℝ ∞ f (extChartAt I x).target ∧
    (∀ y ∈ (extChartAt I x).target, 0 < f y) ∧
    Measure.map (extChartAt I x) (ν.restrict (extChartAt I x).source) =
      (volume.withDensity (fun y => ENNReal.ofReal (f y))).restrict
        (extChartAt I x).target

/-- A smooth positive-volume model with a specified Euclidean or half-space
manifold model. All model dimensions will be quantified below. -/
def HasModelWith {X : Type*} [MeasurableSpace X]
    (μ : Measure X) (T : X → X) {d : ℕ} {H : Type} [TopologicalSpace H]
    (I : ModelWithCorners ℝ (EuclideanSpace ℝ (Fin d)) H) : Prop :=
  ∃ (M : Type) (_ : TopologicalSpace M) (_ : MeasurableSpace M)
      (_ : BorelSpace M) (_ : T2Space M) (_ : SecondCountableTopology M)
      (_ : CompactSpace M) (_ : ChartedSpace H M) (_ : IsManifold I ∞ M)
      (ν : Measure M) (_ : IsProbabilityMeasure ν)
      (S : Diffeomorph I I M M ∞),
    SmoothPositiveDensity I ν ∧ MeasurePreserving S ν ν ∧
      ConullConjugate μ T ν S

/-- Closed manifolds of all dimensions (including zero), and smooth manifolds
with boundary of every positive finite dimension. Nonorientable manifolds
are included: no orientability hypothesis occurs. -/
def HasSmoothPositiveVolumeModel {X : Type*} [MeasurableSpace X]
    (μ : Measure X) (T : X → X) : Prop :=
  (∃ d : ℕ, HasModelWith μ T (𝓡 d)) ∨
    (∃ d : ℕ, ∃ _ : NeZero d, HasModelWith μ T (𝓡∂ d))

/-- A finite-entropy standard nonatomic ergodic system with no smooth positive-volume model. -/
theorem main :
    ∃ (X : Type) (_ : MeasurableSpace X) (μ : Measure X)
      (_ : IsProbabilityMeasure μ) (T : X ≃ᵐ X),
      StandardNonatomic μ ∧ Ergodic T μ ∧ FiniteKSEntropy μ T ∧
        ¬ HasSmoothPositiveVolumeModel μ T := by
  sorry

end SmoothObstruction

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct an ergodic invertible transformation of a standard nonatomic probability space with zero Kolmogorov–Sinai entropy that has no smooth positive-volume model. More precisely, it is not measurably conjugate to any <i>C</i><sup>∞</sup> diffeomorphism preserving a strictly positive smooth probability density on a compact finite-dimensional manifold. A single example excludes every finite dimension, including models on nonorientable manifolds and manifolds with smooth boundary.
